commit 7a1773d9131a1be2d02336654563aded22405da0
Merge: ecad8518 3ea16930

merge: PAGE-KEYS-SETTINGS-COLUMN (fix/p5-page-keys-settings-column @ 3ea16930) into train 14

The merge carries one unit, its 15 commits since its base 3d3d56c8
(INPUT-ROW-LIMIT's head, on main): PAGE-KEYS-SETTINGS-COLUMN 3ea16930.

Conflicts, each resolved by a script in .prd/tui-phase5/receipts/train-14/:
- docs/parity.md, the blocks' rows (rowsmerge.py --drop): one edit
  dropped, CTRLO-OTHER-PICKERS' `/settings` sentence that the menu binds no
  `C-j` (SETTINGS-CJ-CK), which this unit's binding makes false.
- scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml, 13 blocks: 12 `numbers` (blocks.py);
  one by hand (byhand.py): the stack's deleted `at-ranking` and
  `at-folder-row` stay deleted, the unit's new `at-ranking-anchor` kept.
- scripts/fxcap/capfile.py, 1 block, `numbers` (blocks.py).
- src/tui/shell.rs, 5 blocks, by hand (byhand.py), ours in all five: both
  sides put `anchor_at` on the caret's row; CTRLO-OTHER-PICKERS' one anchor
  function kept, the unit's `column_anchor` route not restored.

Semantic fixes (semantic.py 3):
- behaviour: the unit's C-j/C-k check under the full transcript offered
  the keys to the `@` list first; CTRLO-OTHER-PICKERS had moved the list's
  refusal out of `offer_to_files`, so the list moved under Ctrl-O again.
  The call goes; both keys go to the composer, as CTRLO-AT-KEYS has it.
- build: the unit's test helper `walked<T>` is `rows_walked` (the stack's
  `walked` has the name), and three of its tests take main's `CatalogDue`.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

Three differences remain: a failed reading costs xfx one read more at most, and none without an ask, where it can cost fx two (fx stamps a read's start with the ask's own reading, `last_attempt_ms = now`, `:636`; xfx with a reading of its own) -- and one taken on the band's thread sends the runtime an ask its own reading may find nothing due for, after which activity asks no more until the runtime next answers: fewer reads, not more; the two-second wait below counts awake time (tokio's timer), where fx's counts its catalog clock (`model_cache_runtime.zig:849`) -- the two differ only when the machine sleeps inside those two seconds; and a `/model <query>` that the held rows do not match goes as typed, where fx's reads the catalog once more for it, a one-shot beside the cache (`resolveModelQuery` -> `fetchModelIds`, `session_commands.zig:1290-1309`, `src/main.zig:1996-2005`). While a failure's hold holds -- by the readings above -- `/model` opens the menu over the failure at once and asks nothing; past it the menu says `Loading models…` while it reads again behind the failure (`refreshMenuLocked`, `:1046-1050`). **A turn whose request is planned from the catalog** -- a model whose window nothing else gives, a level, Fast or Ultra, the prompt's own images (`requiresResolvedRequestCapabilities`, `orchestrator.zig:5658-5670`, at the turn's start, `:5884-5893`) -- waits two seconds at most for the retry behind a failed catalog that is on its way, such as the one its prompt started, and goes without it after them (`resolveForRequest`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:843-866`, `failed_catalog_retry_wait_ms`, `:45-47`); and once a turn more, at the first request carrying images -- the prompt's, the history's or a tool result's -- while the model is still unknown, the image route asks again and waits as long (`onRequest`, `src/core/images/image_route.zig:180-184`; ResolveOncePerTurn, `orchestrator.zig:7502-7509`). No other request waits. **A stop during the turn's own wait** -- a first load's or a retry's, for the window, a level, a speed or the prompt's own images, which wait for a first load at the turn's start whatever the window (`orchestrator.zig:5885-5886`) -- ends the turn as upstream's does: interrupted, the stop's one `Cancelled` line and no error, its prompt and images kept as asked and stopped, and no refusal said for what the catalog could not tell, Ultra's or an image's (`resolveForRequest`'s cancel, `model_cache_runtime.zig:855-865`; `orchestrator.zig:5893-5919`, and the check after the wait, `:5923-5948`, ahead of Ultra's refusal at the request, `:7657`). **One in the image route's wait at the first request is not copied**: upstream's `try` there (`orchestrator.zig:7508`) ends the turn failed, keeps nothing (`:6092-6134`; `finishExecutionOnlyFailureIfNeeded`, `finalization.zig:226-241`) and says `request failed: Cancelled` under `system` (`:5616-5626`; `app_process_runtime.zig:157-169`, admitted after the stop, `app_worker_runtime.zig:386`); xfx keeps nothing either and ends it as the stop, without the line. **The TUI reads the catalog in the background at launch and after a provider switch**, as upstream warms its model cache (`startModelCacheWarmup`, `src/main.zig:2007-2041@481104fd`, from `app_entry_runtime.zig:340` and `app_auth_runtime.zig:2015-2024`): its answer -- like every read's -- reaches the band the moment it arrives, a turn running or not, as upstream's interface takes a load's end on its next tick (`pollLoadTransition`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:745-760`, from `src/main.zig:2878-2880`); a turn that needs it waits for it. A read is stopped and waited for when a provider switch replaces it, so no old provider's rows follow the switch. **The catalogs**: a llmux daemon's `GET /models`, and the Gateway's `GET {origin}/coding-agent/v1/models` -- the origin of the completion endpoint, so the key goes nowhere a turn does not -- with the turn's bearer, no redirect followed, **read whole or not at all** (CATALOG-BOUNDS): within fx's 30 s, over the whole request from connect to the last byte, and within fx's 16 MiB -- a cap on the body's length as it is read off the wire, an answer of exactly 16 MiB read; not a ceiling on memory: the buffer can grow to just under twice that (`Vec` doubling), and the parsed document (`serde_json::Value`) is a multiple of it, as fx too parses the whole answer (`gateway_json_fetch_timeout_ms`, `max_gateway_json_bytes`, `src/gateway/client.zig:486-509,597-635@481104fd`). Reaching the deadline -- connecting, waiting for the head, or mid-body -- is a failure fx asks again about, and the menu says `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.`; an answer past 16 MiB, declared or grown into, is refused rather than cut to fit, a malformed answer (`catalogRequestFailure`, `src/builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2533@481104fd`), so the menu says `Unable to load models.` and, like any failed catalog, it is asked again only at the next ask past its hold, as above; a body that stops partway, or is not UTF-8, is no catalog either. xfx gives the connect alone 10 s inside the 30 (fx gives it the whole 30): either way a retried failure, in the same words. A request that got no answer at all -- refused, reset, a name that does not resolve -- xfx also says it retries, where fx asks again only after its deadline, a reset or a closing connection (`isRetryableGatewayError`, `src/gateway/client.zig:19-23@481104fd`) and says `Unable to load models.` for the rest, a refused connection among them: MODELS-REASK's choice, unchanged here. **The llmux daemon's catalog has bounds of xfx's own** -- 3 s to connect, 5 s in all, 1 MiB, read the same way, whole or not at all -- because fx has no llmux and the daemon is on this machine; an answer past 1 MiB or not UTF-8 is a malformed catalog (`check the daemon is an llmux version compatible with xfx`), and one that never came whole -- no connection, a failed status (404, 503), a body cut short -- is an unavailable one (`check the daemon is running`); `xfx setup llmux`'s two probes read the same way. parsed for upstream's fields (language rows only; id, vendor, release, `tool-use`, efforts, context window, output ceiling, and Fast/Ultra from `fast_options` or the priced service tiers). A Gateway with no credential has no catalog: upstream falls back to the public one, and xfx does not contact a remote host it was given no key for. With one word it is upstream's router form: the runtime resolves it against the catalog -- loading it first when none is loaded -- an id or an alias spelled exactly, else upstream's fuzzy score (substring, every word, letters in order, any word) over the ids in menu order -- the router's own, which v0.0.16 kept as c1db919 had it (`fuzzyModelScore` and `resolveModelQueryFromIds`, `session_commands.zig:1425-1448,1760@481104fd`, unchanged) while the menu and the column moved to the ranks above -- else the word as typed, **which is applied rather than refused**, as upstream applies an id its catalog does not list. When the band holds the catalog it resolves the word itself, by the same rule (`provider::model::resolve_in_order`), and sends the id -- so the change is announced the moment it is given, as upstream's is; with no catalog in hand the word goes as typed and the runtime's answer is the announcement. **A resolved id is held to the typed word's rule** (`provider::model::model_id_problem`: one word, at most 200 bytes, no control character, C1 included): a catalog id that breaks it is refused, not adopted -- by the runtime, and by the band before it announces an id it resolved or a row it picked -- and the refusal is xfx's own words, never the id. So is every model the profile holds: a settings `model` or `models.<provider>` that breaks the rule is dropped at load with the `invalid_value` diagnostic, and no writer puts one there (`/model`, a speed save, `xfx setup` and `/setup`, `/provider`), `xfx setup llmux` passing over a catalog entry whose name breaks it and refusing a catalog with none that keeps it. Upstream adopts a resolved id (`setResolvedModelRuntime`, `session_commands.zig:1256-1260` at `c1db9191`, `:1399` at `481104fd`) and refuses only the writes that would persist it -- the session's (`validateModel`, `session_codec.zig:3165` at `c1db9191`, `:3830` at `481104fd`) and the settings' (`validateUserPatch`, `putModelPreference`, `settings_store.zig:921,1633`) -- and refuses such a model when it reads settings (`config_runtime.zig:1645,1674`); xfx refuses it at adoption as well, which is unsafe to copy, because the catalog is the provider's document, its parser takes any non-empty id, and an id adopted is announced and painted at once. **The inline column** (`input_completion_runtime.zig:1123-1295`): `/model ` and a space open the catalog's models under the argument -- from the column the argument starts at, six rows at most, the marked row in the bold hint grey and the rest dim -- and the text after it searches them as the menu does, the same ranks in the catalog's order and the first 32 of them (`modelCompletions`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:895-913@481104fd`), so `/model gpt6` lists `openai/gpt-6-astra`, the mark starting on the first ranked row -- and Tab, which writes the marked row, puts it back there, as fx's replacement does (`composer_text_replacement.zig:73-92`), since the line written can list fuzzy matches under its own row; a space typed after a query that is not a model's exact id is not inserted, as in fx (the Space branch, `app_input_runtime.zig:1355-1363@481104fd`, inserts nothing when `advanceModelPickerOnSpace`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:1210-1219`, finds no exact id), so a query of several words reaches the column pasted; Up and Down wrap, Tab writes the marked row into the composer, Escape puts the column away until the line stops being a `/model` line, and Enter on a model with reasoning levels writes `/model <id> ` and opens the **effort column** under the next token: `default`, then the catalog's levels in its order, filtered by what is typed, Enter taking the level spelled out or the marked one -- and, for a model whose row offers Fast, writing `/model <id> <level> ` and opening the **speed column** under the next token (`normal`, then `fast` and `ultrafast` where the row offers them, `fast` marked), whose Enter makes the change; for a model without Fast the level's Enter makes the change, keeping the session's Fast only as far as the row offers it -- so a model that offers Ultra and not Fast cannot be given Ultra from the column, as upstream's cannot (`submitModelPicker`, `:1183`). fx v0.0.16 opens the same effort column on its own for an `/effort ` line, over the model in force and with no column before it (the `/effort` row). A model with no levels and a speed opens the speed column at once (`/model <id> auto `); one with neither is applied from the model column at once. The speed column marks a speed rather than a row (a typed speed resets it to `normal`), Tab writes all three tokens, Left goes back to the levels with the session's own speed marked, and a level spelled out and a space opens it for a model with either speed. Left steps back a column. A space at the column is the column's: after an id spelled exactly it is Enter -- so a typed `/model <id> <more>` for a model with no levels and no speed makes the change and sends the rest as a prompt, as upstream's does -- after a level spelled exactly it opens the speed column for a model with a speed, and otherwise it does nothing. Typing or a Backspace at the end of the effort column keeps it; any other edit, a paste included, goes back to the models. Without rows the column says `loading models...` (and asks the runtime for them, as a control message, whatever the queue holds), `unable to load models` -- and the next frame that draws it asks again once the failure's hold (above) is over, as upstream's render path lists the column's models through `ensureModelCache` (`app_render_runtime.zig:559-568@481104fd`, `src/main.zig:2167-2169`), saying `unable to load models` still while that read is on its way, as upstream's column reads the cache's state, which a retry leaves `.failed` (`app_render_runtime.zig:752-753`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:633-634`), so the column turns into the rows when a read succeeds; an idle session paints no frame and asks nothing, as upstream's does -- `no matching models`, `no matching effort` or `no matching mode`; while a model is pending the hint row previews it, the marked level and the marked speed -- `⚡︎` for Fast from the moment the levels open on a model that offers it, as upstream's does. Enter on a menu row goes on into the effort column for a model with levels (`beginExactModelSelection`) and applies one without. **The explicit form** `/model <id> <effort>`: a literal id, never fuzzy-resolved, and an effort the catalog lists for it or `auto`/`default`; it is applied with that effort and announced; a third word is the speed -- `fast` or `ultrafast` where the row offers it, `normal` where it offers either to decline -- and two words mean `normal`; anything else -- four words, an unlisted effort, a speed the row does not offer -- is upstream's `✗ usage: /model <id> <effort> [normal|fast|ultrafast]`, with the composer kept. **The reasoning effort** is one per session, carried across model changes, saved as `effort` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` (profile-only, `auto` for the default) beside `models.<provider>` in the same write, and -- by the TUI; `xfx ask` and the line-oriented shell read the key but send no effort yet -- sent with every request to a model whose catalog row lists it, the catalog read before the first such turn when nothing has read it: the Gateway's v4 route as a top-level `"reasoning"` (`max` sent as `xhigh`, the route's top tier), llmux as `output_config.effort`, the field every llmux route reads it from; a model the catalog does not describe is sent none. **The speed** -- Fast and Ultra, exclusive, both off whenever the model changes by a query, and held to what the row offers by a pick -- is saved as `fast_mode`, `ultrafast_mode` and `fast_mode_model_bound` (profile-only) beside every model and speed change, and sent by the TUI on the Gateway as upstream's `providerOptions`: `{"gateway":{"speed":"fast"}}` for Fast on a row that offers it, `{"gateway":{"only":["openai"]},"openai":{"serviceTier":"ultrafast"}}` for Ultra -- which, with no silent downgrade, refuses the turn unless the provider is the Gateway, the id is `openai/…` and the row offers it. **llmux reads no per-request speed** (its Fast is the daemon's own codex setting, `2lab-ai/llmux src/config/schema.rs:545-550`, and its catalog publishes no speed), so no llmux row offers one, the speed column never opens there, and nothing is sent. The announcement is upstream's `* Switched to <id> (effort: <level or default>, speed: <normal|fast|ultrafast>)` -- `fast` also for an id ending `-fast` -- or `* Next turn will use …` while a turn runs or waits; the hint row's compact label follows the model in force, with the level after it when the model takes it, then `⚡︎` -- in the row's grey for Fast (bound to the model, or an id ending `-fast`), in upstream's yellow (`38;2;255;204;0`, `38;5;220` at 256 colours) for Ultra. **A change is a control message**, never refused for a full queue: while a turn runs -- or a permission or model question waits, which it does not answer -- it is put aside and applied the moment that turn ends, before anything queued behind it. One put aside while `/provider` switched is saved for the provider it was chosen under and lands on no selector of the new one (upstream refuses the switch while work runs, so its end state is the same), with a warning that says so. A failed catalog load says upstream's `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` (or `AI Gateway rate limited model discovery; xfx retries automatically.`) where asking again could help -- upstream's words since 14a12fea, its own name read as xfx's -- and `Unable to load models.` otherwise (`retryableFailureText`, `model_menu_presentation.zig:435-443@481104fd`); either is read again past its hold, as above. Upstream's third retryable sentence, `Could not refresh model catalog; xfx retries automatically.` in xfx's name, is for any other failure asking again could change: a catalog read that could be given no thread, or no runtime to run on (upstream's `.runtime`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:537-546`), told the moment it is found, a turn running or not, and read again past its hold like any other. A read past its deadline is upstream's retryable `.transport` (`catalogRequestFailure`, `builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2519`, its test `:2545-2547`) -- `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` -- which xfx says for a read whose send times out, or whose body is still coming at the deadline (`GatewayCatalog::fetch`, CATALOG-BOUNDS). The choice is saved as `models.<provider>` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` -- with `provider`, the speed keys and, for a pick (a menu row, the column, the explicit form, a `C-p` row, `/settings`' model list) of a model whose row lists levels, `effort` -- the one it chose or the session's -- as upstream's `selectModelFromPicker` writes them (`session_commands.zig:886-915`; a query writes no effort, `setResolvedModel`, `:1172-1188`) -- on the runtime thread, through the profile's staged `0600` writer, every other key kept, and read back at the next launch under the usual precedence (`XFX_MODEL` over the profile over the compiled default); when a higher layer will still decide a fresh session's model it says `* model: saved to user settings (scope=user); fresh sessions here use higher-precedence model=<layer>` -- `model picker:` for a change made in a column, the menu or the explicit form -- and a failed write `✗ model: active for this process but not saved to user settings (…)`. Every settings write -- a preference, and `xfx setup`'s, which merges the selection it decided into the file as it stands under the lock -- is one read-modify-write under an in-process lock and upstream's cross-process `settings.lock` beside the file, so `/statusline`, `/model` and a setup writing at once all survive. A durable `preferences_changed` event records the model, and the effort and both switches with every change to them -- and at the session's first save, which is the first prompt or a `/rename` before it, the ones in force, right behind `session_started`: a change made before then had no log to go into, and upstream holds every setting made before the first turn and writes them with it (`hold`, `writeFirstTurn`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:415-429`, `:503-539@481104fd`) -- and a resume -- at launch or from the picker -- puts the session's effort and speed back, as upstream's `restoreRuntimePreferences` does. **The line-oriented shell** keeps its own report: bare, the model, the provider and the layer that chose it, and the provider's catalog in the order it was published, bounded at 100 rows with an explicit `... and N more` line; with an argument the same resolution against a catalog it has loaded (an id it does not list is applied, not refused), the shared caveat (`provider::model::unverified_notice`) when no catalog could be consulted, and no profile write. **What a model id may be is one question with one answer on both surfaces**: `provider::model::model_id_problem` -- one printable word of at most 200 bytes, because the id becomes an HTTP header and a session-log field -- asked before the runtime is told anything. Upstream's Ctrl-P model picker is not here. The standalone `models` and `provider` commands remain separate deferred rows. `commands.zig:452`, `model_menu_presentation.zig`, `session_commands.zig:1147-1279`. |

`startWarmup`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:519-551`; `pollLoadTransition`, `:745-760`) -- and a failed load says `Unable to load models.`, an empty catalog `No models available.`, a search that finds nothing `No models found.`. **With rows in hand it lists them at once and asks for them again behind them**, which the runtime reads when the last read began five seconds ago or more (`picker_revalidate_floor_ms`, `:37-38`): the open menu takes the new rows when they land and **keeps its tab and its marked model** -- a tab the new rows do not show falls back to `All`, a model they do not list leaves the mark at the same place, and the window moves with the mark (`hydrateMenuSnapshot`, `:1121-1146`); a read behind the rows that fails keeps them, and the menu says `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` (or the rate-limit sentence) in the note's place when asking again could help (`applyFetchLocked`, `:969-975`; `loadedCatalogStatusText`, `model_menu_presentation.zig:403-404`). **When the catalog is read again** is upstream's model cache's rule (`beginLoad`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:570-648`), and every trigger is the user's own activity, so an idle session never reads it (`:27-28`): the launch and a provider switch read it; the menu opening reads a catalog that failed, and rows five seconds after the last read began; a submitted prompt or steer -- asked as it is submitted, ahead of the work, even behind a running turn (`noteModelCatalogActivity`, `src/main.zig:1201`, `:1225-1232`) --, a frame that draws the inline column (`modelCompletions` -> `ensureModelCache`, `src/main.zig:2167-2169`, from the render path, `app_render_runtime.zig:564`: 36e8c6fc moved only the prompt's hook, and the column's stays), and a resume from the picker or a fork (`startResumedSessionWarmup`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2048-2050`, `:2065`, `:2123`) read a catalog that failed, and rows five minutes after the read that brought them began (the Gateway's `refresh_interval_ms`, `builtins/gateway.zig:2348`, counted from `last_attempt_ms`, set as a read starts, `:609-611`, `:636`; llmux, which upstream has not, is held to the same) or once a read behind them failed in a way asking again could change; one it could not change leaves them to the five minutes, counted from that read's start, and leaves the menu's note as it was (`loadedCatalogStatusText` then reads the loaded catalog's provenance, `model_menu_presentation.zig:405-433`). The runtime tells the band, with every answer, when its clock next owes the catalog a read and when the last read began, and the band asks by the runtime's own rule. `/settings`, `/fast` and `/ultrafast` read the rows the band holds and **start no refresh and no retry**, as upstream's read the cached capabilities (`settingsCatalogSnapshot` -> `resolvedModelCapabilities`, `app_commands.zig:3230-3246`, `src/main.zig:2060-2066`); with no answer yet they wait for the first load the launch or a provider switch owes -- starting it when nothing has, never a refresh. **After a failure, nothing reads it for 5 s, then 10 s, 20 s and so on up to five minutes after each failure in a row**, whatever asks -- by clock readings that succeed and do not go back past the last read's start (both below) --, counted from when the failure arrived; a success clears the count (`failureBackoffMs`, `:40-54`; `completeFetch`, `:936-958`); a provider switch starts over. **The clock counts the time the machine sleeps**, as fx's does (`clockMs`, `std.Io.Clock.boot`, `src/core/gateway/model_catalog.zig:72-79@481104fd`): `CLOCK_MONOTONIC_RAW` on macOS and `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` on Linux, the clocks Zig's `boot` reads (zig 0.16.0 `std/Io/Threaded.zig`, `clockToPosix`), so after a laptop sleep the catalog is as old as the sleep made it and due again on the next ask, and a failure's hold ends in the sleep too; neither moves with the wall clock. A reading earlier than the last read's start is due, whatever held the read back (`beginLoad`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:611-617`), and a reading that failed is the clock's origin, as Zig's `nowPosix` makes fx's (`std/Io/Threaded.zig:11428-11435` at zig 0.16.0; fx pins 0.17.0), so it is due too. Request deadlines stay on the awake clock, as fx's do (`.clock = .awake`, `builtins/gateway.zig:878`, `:1163`). Three differences remain: a failed reading costs xfx one read more at most, and none without an ask, where it can cost fx two (fx stamps a read's start with the ask's own reading, `last_attempt_ms = now`, `:636`; xfx with a reading of its own) -- and one taken on the band's thread sends the runtime an ask its own reading may find nothing due for, after which activity asks no more until the runtime next answers: fewer reads, not more; the two-second wait below counts awake time (tokio's timer), where fx's counts its catalog clock (`model_cache_runtime.zig:849`) -- the two differ only when the machine sleeps inside those two seconds; and a `/model <query>` that the held rows do not match goes as typed, where fx's reads the catalog once more for it, a one-shot beside the cache (`resolveModelQuery` -> `fetchModelIds`, `session_commands.zig:1290-1309`, `src/main.zig:1996-2005`). While a failure's hold holds -- by the readings above -- `/model` opens the menu over the failure at once and asks nothing; past it the menu says `Loading models…` while it reads again behind the failure (`refreshMenuLocked`, `:1046-1050`). **A turn whose request is planned from the catalog** -- a model whose window nothing else gives, a level, Fast or Ultra, the prompt's own images (`requiresResolvedRequestCapabilities`, `orchestrator.zig:5658-5670`, at the turn's start, `:5884-5893`) -- waits two seconds at most for the retry behind a failed catalog that is on its way, such as the one its prompt started, and goes without it after them (`resolveForRequest`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:843-866`, `failed_catalog_retry_wait_ms`, `:45-47`); and once a turn more, at the first request carrying images -- the prompt's, the history's or a tool result's -- while the model is still unknown, the image route asks again and waits as long (`onRequest`, `src/core/images/image_route.zig:180-184`; ResolveOncePerTurn, `orchestrator.zig:7502-7509`). No other request waits. **A stop during the turn's own wait** -- a first load's or a retry's, for the window, a level, a speed or the prompt's own images, which wait for a first load at the turn's start whatever the window (`orchestrator.zig:5885-5886`) -- ends the turn as upstream's does: interrupted, the stop's one `Cancelled` line and no error, its prompt and images kept as asked and stopped, and no refusal said for what the catalog could not tell, Ultra's or an image's (`resolveForRequest`'s cancel, `model_cache_runtime.zig:855-865`; `orchestrator.zig:5893-5919`, and the check after the wait, `:5923-5948`, ahead of Ultra's refusal at the request, `:7657`). **One in the image route's wait at the first request is not copied**: upstream's `try` there (`orchestrator.zig:7508`) ends the turn failed, keeps nothing (`:6092-6134`; `finishExecutionOnlyFailureIfNeeded`, `finalization.zig:226-241`) and says `request failed: Cancelled` under `system` (`:5616-5626`; `app_process_runtime.zig:157-169`, admitted after the stop, `app_worker_runtime.zig:386`); xfx keeps nothing either and ends it as the stop, without the line. **The TUI reads the catalog in the background at launch and after a provider switch**, as upstream warms its model cache (`startModelCacheWarmup`, `src/main.zig:2007-2041@481104fd`, from `app_entry_runtime.zig:340` and `app_auth_runtime.zig:2015-2024`): its answer -- like every read's -- reaches the band the moment it arrives, a turn running or not, as upstream's interface takes a load's end on its next tick (`pollLoadTransition`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:745-760`, from `src/main.zig:2878-2880`); a turn that needs it waits for it. A read is stopped and waited for when a provider switch replaces it, so no old provider's rows follow the switch. **The catalogs**: a llmux daemon's `GET /models`, and the Gateway's `GET {origin}/coding-agent/v1/models` -- the origin of the completion endpoint, so the key goes nowhere a turn does not -- with the turn's bearer, no redirect followed, **read whole or not at all** (CATALOG-BOUNDS): within fx's 30 s, over the whole request from connect to the last byte, and within fx's 16 MiB -- a cap on the body's length as it is read off the wire, an answer of exactly 16 MiB read; not a ceiling on memory: the buffer can grow to just under twice that (`Vec` doubling), and the parsed document (`serde_json::Value`) is a multiple of it, as fx too parses the whole answer (`gateway_json_fetch_timeout_ms`, `max_gateway_json_bytes`, `src/gateway/client.zig:486-509,597-635@481104fd`). Reaching the deadline -- connecting, waiting for the head, or mid-body -- is a failure fx asks again about, and the menu says `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.`; an answer past 16 MiB, declared or grown into, is refused rather than cut to fit, a malformed answer (`catalogRequestFailure`, `src/builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2533@481104fd`), so the menu says `Unable to load models.` and, like any failed catalog, it is asked again only at the next ask past its hold, as above; a body that stops partway, or is not UTF-8, is no catalog either. xfx gives the connect alone 10 s inside the 30 (fx gives it the whole 30): either way a retried failure, in the same words. A request that got no answer at all -- refused, reset, a name that does not resolve -- xfx also says it retries, where fx asks again only after its deadline, a reset or a closing connection (`isRetryableGatewayError`, `src/gateway/client.zig:19-23@481104fd`) and says `Unable to load models.` for the rest, a refused connection among them: MODELS-REASK's choice, unchanged here. **The llmux daemon's catalog has bounds of xfx's own** -- 3 s to connect, 5 s in all, 1 MiB, read the same way, whole or not at all -- because fx has no llmux and the daemon is on this machine; an answer past 1 MiB or not UTF-8 is a malformed catalog (`check the daemon is an llmux version compatible with xfx`), and one that never came whole -- no connection, a failed status (404, 503), a body cut short -- is an unavailable one (`check the daemon is running`); `xfx setup llmux`'s two probes read the same way. parsed for upstream's fields (language rows only; id, vendor, release, `tool-use`, efforts, context window, output ceiling, and Fast/Ultra from `fast_options` or the priced service tiers). A Gateway with no credential has no catalog: upstream falls back to the public one, and xfx does not contact a remote host it was given no key for. With one word it is upstream's router form: the runtime resolves it against the catalog -- loading it first when none is loaded -- an id or an alias spelled exactly, else upstream's fuzzy score (substring, every word, letters in order, any word) over the ids in menu order -- the router's own, which v0.0.16 kept as c1db919 had it (`fuzzyModelScore` and `resolveModelQueryFromIds`, `session_commands.zig:1425-1448,1760@481104fd`, unchanged) while the menu and the column moved to the ranks above -- else the word as typed, **which is applied rather than refused**, as upstream applies an id its catalog does not list. When the band holds the catalog it resolves the word itself, by the same rule (`provider::model::resolve_in_order`), and sends the id -- so the change is announced the moment it is given, as upstream's is; with no catalog in hand the word goes as typed and the runtime's answer is the announcement. **A resolved id is held to the typed word's rule** (`provider::model::model_id_problem`: one word, at most 200 bytes, no control character, C1 included): a catalog id that breaks it is refused, not adopted -- by the runtime, and by the band before it announces an id it resolved or a row it picked -- and the refusal is xfx's own words, never the id. So is every model the profile holds: a settings `model` or `models.<provider>` that breaks the rule is dropped at load with the `invalid_value` diagnostic, and no writer puts one there (`/model`, a speed save, `xfx setup` and `/setup`, `/provider`), `xfx setup llmux` passing over a catalog entry whose name breaks it and refusing a catalog with none that keeps it. Upstream adopts a resolved id (`setResolvedModelRuntime`, `session_commands.zig:1256-1260` at `c1db9191`, `:1399` at `481104fd`) and refuses only the writes that would persist it -- the session's (`validateModel`, `session_codec.zig:3165` at `c1db9191`, `:3830` at `481104fd`) and the settings' (`validateUserPatch`, `putModelPreference`, `settings_store.zig:921,1633`) -- and refuses such a model when it reads settings (`config_runtime.zig:1645,1674`); xfx refuses it at adoption as well, which is unsafe to copy, because the catalog is the provider's document, its parser takes any non-empty id, and an id adopted is announced and painted at once. **The inline column** (`input_completion_runtime.zig:1123-1295`): `/model ` and a space open the catalog's models under the argument -- from the column the argument starts at, six rows at most, the marked row in the bold hint grey and the rest dim -- and the text after it searches them as the menu does, the same ranks in the catalog's order and the first 32 of them (`modelCompletions`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:895-913@481104fd`), so `/model gpt6` lists `openai/gpt-6-astra`, the mark starting on the first ranked row -- and Tab, which writes the marked row, puts it back there, as fx's replacement does (`composer_text_replacement.zig:73-92`), since the line written can list fuzzy matches under its own row; a space typed after a query that is not a model's exact id is not inserted, as in fx (the Space branch, `app_input_runtime.zig:1355-1363@481104fd`, inserts nothing when `advanceModelPickerOnSpace`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:1210-1219`, finds no exact id), so a query of several words reaches the column pasted; Up and Down (and `C-k`/`C-j`) wrap, PgUp and PgDn move a page of the composer's row cap -- in every column, a page past either end landing on the other end (`navigateModelPicker`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:1072-1096@481104fd`; `fx-p5-page-keys-column-24x120-model-pgdn`, `-effort-pgup`, `-speed-pgdn`) -- Tab writes the marked row into the composer, Escape puts the column away until the line stops being a `/model` line, and Enter on a model with reasoning levels writes `/model <id> ` and opens the **effort column** under the next token: `default`, then the catalog's levels in its order, filtered by what is typed, Enter taking the level spelled out or the marked one -- and, for a model whose row offers Fast, writing `/model <id> <level> ` and opening the **speed column** under the next token (`normal`, then `fast` and `ultrafast` where the row offers them, `fast` marked), whose Enter makes the change; for a model without Fast the level's Enter makes the change, keeping the session's Fast only as far as the row offers it -- so a model that offers Ultra and not Fast cannot be given Ultra from the column, as upstream's cannot (`submitModelPicker`, `:1183`). fx v0.0.16 opens the same effort column on its own for an `/effort ` line, over the model in force and with no column before it (the `/effort` row). A model with no levels and a speed opens the speed column at once (`/model <id> auto `); one with neither is applied from the model column at once. The speed column marks a speed rather than a row (a typed speed resets it to `normal`), Tab writes all three tokens, Left goes back to the levels with the session's own speed marked, and a level spelled out and a space opens it for a model with either speed. Left steps back a column. A space at the column is the column's: after an id spelled exactly it is Enter -- so a typed `/model <id> <more>` for a model with no levels and no speed makes the change and sends the rest as a prompt, as upstream's does -- after a level spelled exactly it opens the speed column for a model with a speed, and otherwise it does nothing. Typing or a Backspace at the end of the effort column keeps it; any other edit, a paste included, goes back to the models. Without rows the column says `loading models...` (and asks the runtime for them, as a control message, whatever the queue holds), `unable to load models` -- and the next frame that draws it asks again once the failure's hold (above) is over, as upstream's render path lists the column's models through `ensureModelCache` (`app_render_runtime.zig:559-568@481104fd`, `src/main.zig:2167-2169`), saying `unable to load models` still while that read is on its way, as upstream's column reads the cache's state, which a retry leaves `.failed` (`app_render_runtime.zig:752-753`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:633-634`), so the column turns into the rows when a read succeeds; an idle session paints no frame and asks nothing, as upstream's does -- `no matching models`, `no matching effort` or `no matching mode`; while a model is pending the hint row previews it, the marked level and the marked speed -- `⚡︎` for Fast from the moment the levels open on a model that offers it, as upstream's does. A row too long for the column's room ends in `…` here, where fx cuts it at the screen's edge and adds nothing (`composePickerOptionRowAnnotated` writes the label with `row_text.appendClipped`, `picker_presentation.zig:849@481104fd`, `row_text.zig:23-53`; `fx-p5-page-keys-anchor-24x60-model-wrapped`, the ledger's `model-column-clip`). Owner: MODEL-COLUMN-CLIP. Enter on a menu row goes on into the effort column for a model with levels (`beginExactModelSelection`) and applies one without. **The explicit form** `/model <id> <effort>`: a literal id, never fuzzy-resolved, and an effort the catalog lists for it or `auto`/`default`; it is applied with that effort and announced; a third word is the speed -- `fast` or `ultrafast` where the row offers it, `normal` where it offers either to decline -- and two words mean `normal`; anything else -- four words, an unlisted effort, a speed the row does not offer -- is upstream's `✗ usage: /model <id> <effort> [normal|fast|ultrafast]`, with the composer kept. **The reasoning effort** is one per session, carried across model changes, saved as `effort` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` (profile-only, `auto` for the default) beside `models.<provider>` in the same write, and -- by the TUI; `xfx ask` and the line-oriented shell read the key but send no effort yet -- sent with every request to a model whose catalog row lists it, the catalog read before the first such turn when nothing has read it: the Gateway's v4 route as a top-level `"reasoning"` (`max` sent as `xhigh`, the route's top tier), llmux as `output_config.effort`, the field every llmux route reads it from; a model the catalog does not describe is sent none. **The speed** -- Fast and Ultra, exclusive, both off whenever the model changes by a query, and held to what the row offers by a pick -- is saved as `fast_mode`, `ultrafast_mode` and `fast_mode_model_bound` (profile-only) beside every model and speed change, and sent by the TUI on the Gateway as upstream's `providerOptions`: `{"gateway":{"speed":"fast"}}` for Fast on a row that offers it, `{"gateway":{"only":["openai"]},"openai":{"serviceTier":"ultrafast"}}` for Ultra -- which, with no silent downgrade, refuses the turn unless the provider is the Gateway, the id is `openai/…` and the row offers it. **llmux reads no per-request speed** (its Fast is the daemon's own codex setting, `2lab-ai/llmux src/config/schema.rs:545-550`, and its catalog publishes no speed), so no llmux row offers one, the speed column never opens there, and nothing is sent. The announcement is upstream's `* Switched to <id> (effort: <level or default>, speed: <normal|fast|ultrafast>)` -- `fast` also for an id ending `-fast` -- or `* Next turn will use …` while a turn runs or waits; the hint row's compact label follows the model in force, with the level after it when the model takes it, then `⚡︎` -- in the row's grey for Fast (bound to the model, or an id ending `-fast`), in upstream's yellow (`38;2;255;204;0`, `38;5;220` at 256 colours) for Ultra. **A change is a control message**, never refused for a full queue: while a turn runs -- or a permission or model question waits, which it does not answer -- it is put aside and applied the moment that turn ends, before anything queued behind it. One put aside while `/provider` switched is saved for the provider it was chosen under and lands on no selector of the new one (upstream refuses the switch while work runs, so its end state is the same), with a warning that says so. A failed catalog load says upstream's `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` (or `AI Gateway rate limited model discovery; xfx retries automatically.`) where asking again could help -- upstream's words since 14a12fea, its own name read as xfx's -- and `Unable to load models.` otherwise (`retryableFailureText`, `model_menu_presentation.zig:435-443@481104fd`); either is read again past its hold, as above. Upstream's third retryable sentence, `Could not refresh model catalog; xfx retries automatically.` in xfx's name, is for any other failure asking again could change: a catalog read that could be given no thread, or no runtime to run on (upstream's `.runtime`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:537-546`), told the moment it is found, a turn running or not, and read again past its hold like any other. A read past its deadline is upstream's retryable `.transport` (`catalogRequestFailure`, `builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2519`, its test `:2545-2547`) -- `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` -- which xfx says for a read whose send times out, or whose body is still coming at the deadline (`GatewayCatalog::fetch`, CATALOG-BOUNDS). The choice is saved as `models.<provider>` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` -- with `provider`, the speed keys and, for a pick (a menu row, the column, the explicit form, a `C-p` row, `/settings`' model list) of a model whose row lists levels, `effort` -- the one it chose or the session's -- as upstream's `selectModelFromPicker` writes them (`session_commands.zig:886-915`; a query writes no effort, `setResolvedModel`, `:1172-1188`) -- on the runtime thread, through the profile's staged `0600` writer, every other key kept, and read back at the next launch under the usual precedence (`XFX_MODEL` over the profile over the compiled default); when a higher layer will still decide a fresh session's model it says `* model: saved to user settings (scope=user); fresh sessions here use higher-precedence model=<layer>` -- `model picker:` for a change made in a column, the menu or the explicit form -- and a failed write `✗ model: active for this process but not saved to user settings (…)`. Every settings write -- a preference, and `xfx setup`'s, which merges the selection it decided into the file as it stands under the lock -- is one read-modify-write under an in-process lock and upstream's cross-process `settings.lock` beside the file, so `/statusline`, `/model` and a setup writing at once all survive. A durable `preferences_changed` event records the model, and the effort and both switches with every change to them -- and at the session's first save, which is the first prompt or a `/rename` before it, the ones in force, right behind `session_started`: a change made before then had no log to go into, and upstream holds every setting made before the first turn and writes them with it (`hold`, `writeFirstTurn`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:415-429`, `:503-539@481104fd`) -- and a resume -- at launch or from the picker -- puts the session's effort and speed back, as upstream's `restoreRuntimePreferences` does. **The line-oriented shell** keeps its own report: bare, the model, the provider and the layer that chose it, and the provider's catalog in the order it was published, bounded at 100 rows with an explicit `... and N more` line; with an argument the same resolution against a catalog it has loaded (an id it does not list is applied, not refused), the shared caveat (`provider::model::unverified_notice`) when no catalog could be consulted, and no profile write. **What a model id may be is one question with one answer on both surfaces**: `provider::model::model_id_problem` -- one printable word of at most 200 bytes, because the id becomes an HTTP header and a session-log field -- asked before the runtime is told anything. Upstream's Ctrl-P model picker is not here. The standalone `models` and `provider` commands remain separate deferred rows. `commands.zig:452`, `model_menu_presentation.zig`, `session_commands.zig:1147-1279`. |
+| `/effort` | slash | implemented | fx v0.0.16's command (`vercel-labs/fx@481104fd`, commit `04a5e3af`): upstream's row, "choose the reasoning effort for the current model", Model, after `/model` (`commands.zig:459`). **The TUI** answers it as upstream does. The current model's catalog row is read first, whatever follows the name: a model whose row lists no level, or a catalog this side does not hold, says `* effort: <model> has no levels to choose from` -- or, while the catalog read is still on its way, `* effort: levels for <model> are still loading; try /effort again in a moment` -- and nothing else happens; nothing is asked of the runtime (`handleEffort`, `session_commands.zig:1000-1013`). Bare, it opens the Effort menu in the band under the composer and writes nothing to the document: `Effort N` in the bold hint grey and the model in the dim grey (its middle given up when it does not fit), a blank row, then `default` and the model's levels in the row's order, the marked one bold and the rest dim, `current` in the dim grey two cells past the widest label on the level in use; it opens on the level in use, or on `default` for one the model does not offer (`effort_menu.zig:30-49`, `effort_menu_presentation.zig`). Ctrl-O over it shows nothing: fx's frame closes the full transcript while it is up (`reconcileBeforeFrameRender`, `app_render_runtime.zig:1206-1215@481104fd`), so every key stays the menu's, and it opening under the screen takes the plane back (`p5-ctrlo-effort`; CTRLO-OTHER-PICKERS). Up and Down (and `C-k`/`C-j`) **wrap**; Page Up/Down move by the composer's row cap and wrap the same way, as fx's do: on a 41-row screen a page is 19 levels, so PgDn from `default` lands on `xhigh`, four on round five (`move`'s `@mod`, `effort_menu.zig:53-57@481104fd`; `fx-p5-input-row-limit-menus-41x120-effort-pgdn`); Enter closes it and applies the marked level; Escape closes it; any edit to the composer -- a keystroke, a paste -- closes it and the edit stands (`app_input_runtime.zig:1984-1990`, `:2869-2884`). Shift-Tab is not the menu's: it cycles the permission mode with the menu up, and the menu stays, as upstream's (`.toggle_permission_mode`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1048-1057`). A model change, a provider switch or a resume that lands while the menu is up closes it, so Enter cannot apply one model's level to another; upstream has no such rule, because its changes are commands and typing one closes the menu. Its key help is `↑↓ navigate     enter use     esc close` and upstream's narrower variants, or `press ctrl+c again to exit` while a Ctrl-C would leave (`input_presentation.zig:671-702`). A short footer gives up the blank row, then the title, before a level, and the levels then scroll with the mark (`effort_menu_presentation.zig:17-59`; the end of the conversation stays in view). With a word, `/effort <level>` applies a level the row lists, spelled as it lists it, or `default`/`auto`/`adaptive` in any case; a well-formed name it does not list says `* effort: <name> is not available for <model>; levels: default, …`, and anything that is not a level name says `* effort: levels for <model>: default, …` without repeating it (`session_commands.zig:1014-1037`). In the TUI the whole of it is a transaction on the runtime thread: the profile's preimage is snapshotted first, every network and read step runs before anything is written, a cancellation may win **only** before the commit, a commit error is classified by rereading the raw bytes rather than by trusting the error value, the configuration is then re-read from the file that was written, and a reload failure rolls back only while the disk still holds exactly the document this transaction wrote -- bytes and Unix mode both. Bytes it did not write are never overwritten: a third state on disk means another writer owns the file, and the session ends rather than clobbering it. What the shell shows afterwards -- the model, the credential fact -- is what the reload resolved to rather than what the write intended, so a settings layer that outranks the profile is named instead of silently winning.  | `/allowlist` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/allowlist [view [effective\|local\|user]\|[local\|user] add\|remove\|reset ...]`, "manage trusted commands, tools, and URLs", Security (`commands.zig:480`), every word case-insensitive (`handleAllowlist`, `session_commands.zig:396-444`). **Two tables, both in `~/.xfx/settings.json`**: `user` is the top-level `permission`, `local` (the default) is `workspaces["<canonical workspace>"].permission` -- never a file in the repository; `permission` is profile-only, so a `.xfx.json` that carries one is reported and ignored. A command is judged by its text as `bash`/`run_command` (so a `terminal` key, upstream's non-run actions, allows no command); a write, an edit or `create_folder` (`edit`) at the target, its parent and every directory it creates, and a read tool (`read_file` `read`, `glob_files` and `list_files` `glob`, `grep_files` `grep`) at the path it resolved -- a search at the directory it searches below -- each canonical (links resolved, case as the filesystem stores it) and workspace-relative. Order of a decision (`tool_admission.zig:1311-1511`): yolo skips every rule; a deny on any target refuses; a saved-session rule for exactly this call decides; a rule that asks prompts **in auto mode too** (with nobody to ask, the refusal names the rule) unless an exact grant answers it; an allow covering every target admits in any mode; then grants, then the mode -- and a read no rule decides is free in every mode. **A rule that asks before a read puts it to the person**, as upstream does (`configured_ask`, `:1426-1442`): the band is upstream's generic one -- `Permission needed · Choose one` with `Tool` on the right, `Would you like to allow this action?`, `Reason: This action needs approval before xfx can continue.` (`Reason:` in 245), the tool and its label argument as the call gave it (`read_file plan.md`; for a search its pattern, `grep_files needle`, `formatPermissionLabel`, `tool_presentation.zig:683-718`), `1.  | `/undo` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/undo`, "undo the latest tracked file operation", Session, no argument (`commands.zig:481`); `/undo now` is an unknown command in the TUI. It takes back the newest change this conversation's own `write_file` or `edit_file` committed -- the file put back as it was, or deleted when the change created it -- and says so in one neutral notice, upstream's words with the absolute path spelled terminal-safe (`\x1b`, `\x0a`, a byte that is not UTF-8 as `\xNN`): `* undo: Restored <path>`, `* undo: Deleted <path> (was newly created)`, `* undo: Could not undo <path>`, `* undo: Nothing to undo.` (`commandUndoLast`, `app_commands.zig:1242-1290`). The line shell prints the same sentence as `[shell] undo: …`. **What is tracked** is upstream's (`ChangeTracker`, `change_tracker.zig:30-99`; `publishCommittedTracker`, `app_callbacks.zig:1125-1170`): a write or an edit that landed, newest last, at most 100 deep with the oldest dropped first -- not `create_folder`, not a command, not a change that was refused, failed or changed nothing -- and a change is taken off whether or not it could be reversed. xfx also drops the oldest past 64 MiB of preimage (upstream has no byte bound; a preimage may be a 10 MiB file), always keeping the newest. **In memory only**, as upstream's: nothing is written to the session log and the model is not told; `/new`, `/clear`, `/reset` and `/resume` leave every earlier change behind (`app_session_runtime.zig:1922`, `:1999`; upstream's resume test is answered `Nothing to undo.`, `tui-resume.test.ts:6029-6037`); a provider switch does not, because it keeps the conversation, as upstream's `/provider` keeps its session and its tracker (`runProviderCommand`, `app_auth_runtime.zig:387`). **No question is asked in any permission mode** -- the person typed it, and upstream's handler calls `undoLast` directly -- **but every structural rule of a write holds**: the target is located again from the conversation's scope (an authorized root, no `..`, nothing under `.git` or `.xfx`, no symbolic link on the way), read without following a link, and replaced by a staged file renamed into its own directory with its permission bits kept, or unlinked there; a directory on the path that has gone is not made again. **Stricter than upstream** in one rule, the read-proof rule every write here obeys: the file must still be what the change left there or what this session last read of it, so a file edited by someone else since, or a link standing where it was, is `Could not undo` and left as it is (upstream restores over whatever is there, `change_tracker.zig:53-81`, `:501-537`). A read proof is left alone, so the model's next edit of a file it has not read as it now is is refused until it reads it again. **In the TUI** it is done on the runtime thread: at once between turns; typed while a turn runs it is done the moment that turn is over (upstream's is done at once) **and takes back the change that was newest when it was typed** -- the band reads the journal's position at the keystroke, and a change the turn makes after it stays for the next `/undo`; if that later change touched the same file, the undo is `Could not undo` and the file is left as the later change made it. One typed after a `/new` the runtime has not reached yet is answered for the fresh conversation. Its answer is never dropped by a reset's drain. |

The entry is the repository's own file, so its text shows nothing the file does not; a credential written into it literally (by a person, or by `/mcp add --project --env`) is shown as the file shows it, and one in xfx's environment never is. Values a person typed are still never said back by the verbs (`typed`), and a profile's are never shown by `list` or `show`: neither is an approval. A server's command line and address -- in `show`, on the menu's screen, and in `approve`'s sentence -- are its file's, `${VAR}` and all, a profile server's and an approved project server's alike: what an expansion puts in is for the server (the profile and project rows; `ServerConfig::shown_command`, `shown_url`), and reaches a screen only if the server prints it to its stderr (the client runtime row). Every row is spelled as the approval view spells a command it asks about (`approval_view::command_text`: every control `\xNN`, a line break included, and each bidirectional, zero-width or invisible code point the table names `\u{NNNN}`, so a `U+202E` cannot draw the command backwards); a row longer than 1024 bytes, which would fill the screen, is its first 512 and last 256 bytes with its length and where it is whole between them (`[N bytes in all; .mcp.json has every byte]`), never cut without saying so -- and such an entry is not shown whole, so Enter approves nothing (`A row of X's entry is too long to show here whole, so nothing was approved: .mcp.json has every byte, and /mcp approve X approves the entry as xfx read it.`). A row the width wraps goes on in rows led by `↳ `, two columns in, where each row of the entry's own is four in -- every wrapped piece, of a command line or address alone too, so none passes for a row the entry has (`v`, a run of spaces, `env: PATH=/usr/bin`; an address `https://a.example/mcp?q=`, spaces, `header: X-Team: blue`, opus-B). The cost: fx's goes on four columns in, so a wrapped command line or address here is led by `↳ ` where fx's is not. (2) It takes the rows it needs past fx's thirteen, up to the space the screen gives a panel, and Enter approves only what a frame drew whole -- every row in the panel's rows and none cut with `…`, as the row measures it, a cluster at a time: one that could not, or that something wrote over since (a refused write, a resize, a screen the band is blind on), approves nothing and says `xfx couldn't show all of what X runs on this screen, so nothing was approved.`. (3) Enter approves the entry that frame drew, by its digest (`TrustAction::ApproveShown`), so what is saved is the entry xfx read and showed, and a reload admits it only while the file still holds it -- a server a reload keeps keeps its screen's entry only while that entry's digest is the fresh read's, so an entry respelled (`"type": "stdio"` added) is shown, and approved, as it now is: a rewrite read before Enter approves nothing (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx showed it; nothing was approved.`, and the screen shows the new one next), and one on disk not yet read is saved as the entry shown, which the next read finds changed and leaves waiting. fx's Approve runs `approve NAME`, which approves what its host holds when the verb runs (`verbs.zig:631-646`). An approval is for that entry, not for its name. Upstream keys it by name alone (`resolveAdmission`, `src/core/mcp/project_config.zig:825-832@c1db9191`, unchanged at `:938-945@481104fd`) while its auto mode writes `.mcp.json` without review like any other workspace file (`src/core/tooling/tool_admission.zig:663-693@c1db9191`, `:662-692@481104fd`): a model's file write could swap an approved server's command or address and have it run on the next reload, unasked. In xfx a changed entry is pending again (MCP project servers and trust, below) and waits for the menu or `approve`. Nothing reloads the MCP configuration without the person -- launch, and an `add`, `remove`, `approve` or `reject`, typed or chosen in the menu, are the only triggers; there is no file watch and no model tool that reloads. The typed `approve --all` keeps fx's behaviour: every entry the last read showed as a pending server, not ones added later (`CHANGELOG.md:165@481104fd`), and not one that parses only once expanded (`"type": "${T:-stdio}"` is shown as a configuration error, not as a server); `approve NAME`, typed, is the one way to approve such an entry: it has no server to find by name, so `approve` approves the entry the runtime last read, bound to that read's digest as the parsed path is, and only while `.mcp.json` still holds it (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx read it; nothing was approved.` otherwise, and fx's `No project server named …` for an entry the runtime never read), saying what it runs or reaches but never its `env` or `headers` -- fx's `approve` finds only servers that parse (`verbs.zig:631-646@481104fd`), so this is xfx's, kept from c1db9191's `trust approve <name>`; `reject NAME` reaches such an entry the same way. An approval is for the entry, not for the workspace code it loads: `node server.js` is approved as that command line, and an edit to `server.js` asks nothing (a later unit looks at that). It runs:` or `It connects to:`, then what it runs or reaches wrapped at spaces and never cut, `enter confirm     esc cancel`; presentation `:437-499`, `:507-515`), spelled before it is wrapped, so a control spelled out wraps as the cells it takes. **Where xfx's differs -- deliberately, because upstream's is unsafe to copy**: (1) it shows what the child is given, from the entry the runtime read (`ShownEntry`: its rows and the digest an approval binds, from one read) -- the command line, each word as a POSIX shell would read it back -- a word xfx expands (`${NAME}`, `${NAME:-default}`) in double quotes, every other `$`, `"`, `\` and `` ` `` escaped, so the reader expands what xfx will, and a control character beside an expansion an ANSI-C piece between quoted ones (`"a"$'\u001B'"${X}"`; one inside a default, which has no exact spelling there, stays in the quotes as the row spells it, `\x1b`) -- or the address; then one row `env: NAME=value` for each variable a stdio entry sets, or `header: Name: value` for each header a remote entry sends (a `header_env` header as `X-Team: ${TEAM}`, `bearer_token_env` as `Authorization: Bearer ${TOKEN}`, as they are sent) -- every value as written, since a pending entry is never expanded: a literal, a default with its text (`${NOPE:-./y}`), a `${VAR}` as the name it is, and never a value from xfx's environment. fx's shows the command line or the address alone (`confirmLines`, presentation `:442-481`). xfx's rule since 2026-11-04 (`c4d252f5`): a variable changes what runs as much as the command does -- `"PATH": "./x:${PATH}"` makes `node` run `./x/node`, since the command is resolved with the child's PATH, and `NODE_OPTIONS`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_*` or `PYTHONPATH` change it as much -- so a key alone hides what is approved. The entry is the repository's own file, so its text shows nothing the file does not; a credential written into it literally (by a person, or by `/mcp add --project --env`) is shown as the file shows it, and one in xfx's environment never is. Values a person typed are still never said back by the verbs (`typed`), and a profile's are never shown by `list` or `show`: neither is an approval. A server's command line and address -- in `show`, on the menu's screen, and in `approve`'s sentence -- are its file's, `${VAR}` and all, a profile server's and an approved project server's alike: what an expansion puts in is for the server (the profile and project rows; `ServerConfig::shown_command`, `shown_url`), and reaches a screen only if the server prints it to its stderr (the client runtime row). Every row is spelled as the approval view spells a command it asks about (`approval_view::command_text`: every control `\xNN`, a line break included, and each bidirectional, zero-width or invisible code point the table names `\u{NNNN}`, so a `U+202E` cannot draw the command backwards); a row longer than 1024 bytes, which would fill the screen, is its first 512 and last 256 bytes with its length and where it is whole between them (`[N bytes in all; .mcp.json has every byte]`), never cut without saying so -- and such an entry is not shown whole, so Enter approves nothing (`A row of X's entry is too long to show here whole, so nothing was approved: .mcp.json has every byte, and /mcp approve X approves the entry as xfx read it.`). A row the width wraps goes on in rows led by `↳ `, two columns in, where each row of the entry's own is four in -- every wrapped piece, of a command line or address alone too, so none passes for a row the entry has (`v`, a run of spaces, `env: PATH=/usr/bin`; an address `https://a.example/mcp?q=`, spaces, `header: X-Team: blue`, opus-B). The cost: fx's goes on four columns in, so a wrapped command line or address here is led by `↳ ` where fx's is not. (2) It takes the rows it needs past fx's thirteen, up to the space the screen gives a panel, and Enter approves only what a frame drew whole -- every row in the panel's rows and none cut with `…`, as the row measures it, a cluster at a time: one that could not, or that something wrote over since (a refused write, a resize, a screen the band is blind on), approves nothing and says `xfx couldn't show all of what X runs on this screen, so nothing was approved.`. (3) Enter approves the entry that frame drew, by its digest (`TrustAction::ApproveShown`), so what is saved is the entry xfx read and showed, and a reload admits it only while the file still holds it -- a server a reload keeps keeps its screen's entry only while that entry's digest is the fresh read's, so an entry respelled (`"type": "stdio"` added) is shown, and approved, as it now is: a rewrite read before Enter approves nothing (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx showed it; nothing was approved.`, and the screen shows the new one next), and one on disk not yet read is saved as the entry shown, which the next read finds changed and leaves waiting. fx's Approve runs `approve NAME`, which approves what its host holds when the verb runs (`verbs.zig:631-646`). An approval is for that entry, not for its name. Upstream keys it by name alone (`resolveAdmission`, `src/core/mcp/project_config.zig:825-832@c1db9191`, unchanged at `:938-945@481104fd`) while its auto mode writes `.mcp.json` without review like any other workspace file (`src/core/tooling/tool_admission.zig:663-693@c1db9191`, `:662-692@481104fd`): a model's file write could swap an approved server's command or address and have it run on the next reload, unasked. In xfx a changed entry is pending again (MCP project servers and trust, below) and waits for the menu or `approve`. Nothing reloads the MCP configuration without the person -- launch, and an `add`, `remove`, `approve` or `reject`, typed or chosen in the menu, are the only triggers; there is no file watch and no model tool that reloads. A `$word` that arrives in a paste -- text, or beside a pasted image -- and names a skill opens it too (`maybeOpenPastedSkillMenu`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:612-663`). **The slash popup** lists the skills its query finds after its commands, in the menu's `All` order, each by its name with no slash, its description and its short source (`workspace .xfx`, `workspace skills/`, `global .xfx`), under `Results N` once a skill is among the rows (`prepareSlashMenu`, `slashMenuRowContent`, `composeSlashMenuHeaderRow`, `picker_presentation.zig:1100-1266`); Tab or Enter on a skill row makes the slash word its token, and Enter runs a command instead when the query is one's exact name (`bindSelectedSlashSkill`, `submitSlashPickerSelection`, `app_input_runtime.zig:2810-2919`). **The ghost after the caret** (`visibleInlineCompletion`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:598-640`): a `$word` ending the draft with no menu showing -- after the space that ends the `$` menu is taken back, say, or on a recalled line -- has the rest of the first skill name it begins (discovery order, ASCII case aside, `firstSkillNameCompletion`) painted after it in the dim grey and closed by a reset (`\e[38;5;245m…\e[0m`, `appendInlineCompletionSuffix`, `input_presentation.zig:955-973`), cut to the cells the row has and not shown when its first character does not fit; a slash word ending a draft that has other words before it has the rest of the first command it begins, in registry order, a name before its aliases (`firstSlashCompletion`, `command_specs.zig:490-506`). A draft that is one slash word is the popup's and has none. Right with nothing selected and Tab take it -- a skill's as its token in place of the `$word`, a command's as the letters with no space -- and Enter sends the draft as typed. Escape dismisses it until the draft stops being that kind of word, as Escape on the `$` menu does its word's; with a turn running, Escape arms the interrupt over a skill's instead. None shows under a question or while `/help`, `/settings`, `/model`'s list, the session picker or a skills menu owns the band, and a recalled slash word shows none until it is edited (`inlineCompletionSurfaceAvailable`, `:703-727`; `picker_state.zig:142-167`, `:451-467`). A token is the skill's **name**, painted bold in the composer and in the card's accent on the card, a space after it when the next character would join a word; it is one unit (a delete over it takes it whole), an edit that takes one or puts one in is an undo boundary as an image badge's is, a kill and a yank carry it, and a recalled line brings it back. The prompt carries it as `$name` (`"text":"$demo run it"`), and the turn **binds** the exact directory the menu chose. **What the model is told**, on every request of every front end (TUI, line shell, `ask`) from the skills discovered as the turn starts: upstream's `<available_skills>` system block before the project instructions, byte for byte -- two sentences, one `Root N: <dir>` per directory, one `- <name>: <description> (location: skill:<namespace>:<root>/<leaf>)` per skill, the namespace upstream's SHA-256 of the advertised names and paths, values written as upstream's model scalars, descriptions cut at 1024 characters, the block kept to upstream's budget -- two percent of the model's context window at four bytes a token (a 200k window is 16000 bytes), from the catalog the TUI reads at launch or, in the line shell and `ask`, from the window the Gateway is known to give the model; 8000 characters when no window is known (`SkillPromptBudget.resolve`, `skill_runtime.zig:3061-3083`) -- by shortening descriptions and then omitting entries from the end with `Omitted skills: N.` (`renderSkillPrompt`, `:3115-3322`) -- and, when the prompt names skills (a bound token, a `$name` outside quotes, code and `@` paths, a leading `/name`, or "use the X skill", not after a negation -- `collectExplicitSkillSelections`, `:2696-2797`), upstream's `Explicitly invoked skill content for this query:` block after the project instructions with each skill's `SKILL.md` whole in `<skill_content name=… location=… resource="SKILL.md" complete="true">` (`buildExplicitPromptSection`, `skill_invocation.zig:205-289`), on that turn's requests only. The TUI says `* 1 requested skill loaded` / `└ Loaded skill demo` under the card, or `! Requested skills · 0 loaded · 1 failed (ctrl+o for details)` with each failure on the full transcript; the line shell says the same rows as `[shell]` lines. A prompt with a token is not steered into a running turn, as upstream's is not (`sameTurnSteeringEligible`). **`path`**: `* skills: xfx workspace roots are auto-discovered from .xfx/skills and skills/.` and `xfx managed install root: <home>/.xfx/skills` -- upstream's third line, about compatibility roots, is not said, xfx reading none. **`create <name>`** writes upstream's template `SKILL.md` into the managed root (`* skills: Created <path>`), a name with `/` or `\` refused in upstream's words. **`remove <name>`** deletes only a managed skill (`* skills: Removed skill 'made'.`); any other is refused naming where it is (`Skill 'demo' comes from workspace .xfx/skills, not the xfx managed install root. Remove it from <path>.`). **`add`/`install <source> [--skill <name>]`** read upstream's pasted forms (`npx skills add …`, `skills.sh/<owner>/<repo>/<skill>`, `<owner>/<repo>@<skill>`) and install **from a local directory**: `* skills: Installing from <source>...`, then `* skills: Installed: <name>` per skill -- a top-level `SKILL.md` under the directory's name, each other one under its own directory's, copied without `.git*` or links into the managed root through a staged directory and two renames under `~/.xfx/.skill-install-locks/<name>` (`copySkillDirWithOptions`). **The second decided default:** a source that is not an existing directory is answered as upstream answers a clone that failed -- `* skills: Failed to install. Check the source path or URL and try again.` -- and nothing reaches the network; upstream would run `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/<source>.git` (`installFromGitHub`, `skills.zig:265-280`). Anything else is `* usage: /skills [list|add|install|show|create|remove|path] [name|url|path]`. **The line-oriented shell** answers the same forms as `[shell] skills: …` lines, the usage line on stderr; bare, `list` and `show` print upstream's listing (`Visible skills (N):`, grouped, `listSkillsSummary`, `skill_runtime.zig:2964-3004`). **Where a link may go** (`canonicalPathHasReadAuthority`, `skill_runtime.zig:586-681`): a link in a workspace root -- the root itself, a candidate, its `SKILL.md` -- is followed only when it resolves inside the workspace ancestor the root hangs off, or inside a directory the user named: the profile's `skill_symlink_authorities` (an array of at most 32 absolute paths with no `..`, profile-only -- a `.xfx.json` that carries it is reported and ignored -- the workspace's own entry replacing the global list, each canonical where it resolves and as written where it does not yet; `config_runtime.zig:1784-1877`) or `XFX_SKILL_SYMLINK_AUTHORITIES` (colon-separated absolute paths, as written, a relative or `..` entry skipped). By default none is named, so a link out of the workspace is refused with fx's diagnostic, and a link in the managed root is never followed. **Fail closed:** an unreadable list in any profile layer names no authority at all (`invalid_value key=skill_symlink_authorities skill_symlink_authorities must be an array of at most 32 absolute directory paths without .. components`), where upstream drops only the layer it could not parse. The authorities are read once, when the configuration is loaded at launch. **Narrower than upstream's**: a candidate's root is found missing through linked directories, where upstream's walk stops at a link. Receipt: `.prd/tui-phase4/receipts/s1/`. |

The fork goes on as a resumed session does -- the provider and model its log recorded as of the cut, unless `XFX_MODEL` names one, and the effort and switches it recorded -- and **its permissions are its own**: the mode in force, never the one the log recorded, as a resume's; the grants its log holds and no other, so an "always" the source took back after the cut, or gave after it, is not the fork's at its next turn either; and the saved-session rules (the `/permissions` row) its own `permissions.json`, holding **each deny the source has remembered**, as the source holds it at the fork with its id, and **no allow**, read by the fork's first call on. **Divergence:** fx v0.0.16 keeps a session's rules as `set permissions` lines in its log (`setPermissions`, `src/core/session/session_adapter.zig:2205-2208@481104fd`), so its fork holds them as they stood at the cut, allows and denies, among the lines 2..S it copies (`openFork`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:1744-1747`; `copyFolded`, `:1837-1868@481104fd`), and restores them as a resume does (`restoreWithPermissionState`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2179@481104fd`). xfx keeps them beside the log, where only what they are now can be read, so its fork takes what never widens what it may do: every deny -- one remembered after the cut too, which fx's fork does not hold -- and no allow, since nothing shows one was in force at the cut: where fx's fork keeps an allow made before the cut, xfx's asks again (in `auto`, refuses what `auto` would not admit on its own). A deny revoked after the cut is not the fork's, as the source no longer holds it; fx's fork holds it. Without the carry a fork lost every remembered deny: in `auto` a write in the workspace or a read-only command it named ran, and in every mode but full access a read it named was read without a question (`tests/tui.rs`'s `a_remembered_deny_still_refuses_its_*_in_a_fork_in_*`). The file is written in the fork's stage with the rest -- owner-only, published with `link(2)`, read back -- so the fork is never seen without it; the source's is read not through a link, and one that cannot be read, or is a link, a folder or a FIFO, refuses the fork (`✗ session: Unable to fork the session.`), as a source whose store cannot be read refuses every call. No child process moves: the MCP servers are the runtime's and the shell tool's commands end with their calls. Its first request carries the kept turns and the prompt (`fx-p5-effort-fork-fork-40x120-bodies`). It is on disk from the fork, so the exit line names it even with no turn (D90, `tests/tla/session_manager/system-design.md:1536-1560`); `/status`'s `· saved` is that row's. **The line-oriented shell** has no menu and no draft to put a prompt back into, says where the menu is, and writes nothing. `app_commands.zig:455-466`, `app_session_runtime.zig:573-600`, `:3948-3958`. |

The runtime tells the band, with every answer, when its clock next owes the catalog a read, and the band asks then. **After a failure, nothing reads it for 5 s, then 10 s, 20 s and so on up to five minutes after each failure in a row**, whatever asks, counted from when the failure arrived; a success clears the count (`failureBackoffMs`, `:40-54`; `completeFetch`, `:936-958`); a provider switch starts over. **The clock is one that stops while the machine sleeps** (Rust's `Instant`: `CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW` on macOS, `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` on Linux), where fx's catalog clock keeps counting through sleep (`clockMs`, `std.Io.Clock.boot`, `src/core/gateway/model_catalog.zig:72-79@481104fd`): both ignore wall-clock changes, but after a laptop sleep fx's catalog is as old as the sleep made it, due again on the next ask, and xfx's is only as old as the time the machine was awake -- and a backoff likewise. Not yet copied (CATALOG-BOUNDS found it; a clock that counts sleep would replace `Instant` across the catalog's state).
