A read is stopped and waited for when a provider switch replaces it, so no old provider's rows follow the switch. **The catalogs**: a llmux daemon's `GET /models`, and the Gateway's `GET {origin}/coding-agent/v1/models` -- the origin of the completion endpoint, so the key goes nowhere a turn does not -- with the turn's bearer, no redirect followed, **read whole or not at all** (CATALOG-BOUNDS): within fx's 30 s, over the whole request from connect to the last byte, and within fx's 16 MiB -- a cap on the body's length as it is read off the wire, an answer of exactly 16 MiB read; not a ceiling on memory: the buffer can grow to just under twice that (`Vec` doubling), and the parsed document (`serde_json::Value`) is a multiple of it, as fx too parses the whole answer (`gateway_json_fetch_timeout_ms`, `max_gateway_json_bytes`, `src/gateway/client.zig:486-509,597-635@481104fd`). Reaching the deadline -- connecting, waiting for the head, or mid-body -- is a failure fx asks again about, and the menu says `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.`; an answer past 16 MiB, declared or grown into, is refused rather than cut to fit, a malformed answer (`catalogRequestFailure`, `src/builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2533@481104fd`), so the menu says `Unable to load models.` and, like any failed catalog, it is asked again only at the next ask after the backoff; a body that stops partway, or is not UTF-8, is no catalog either. xfx gives the connect alone 10 s inside the 30 (fx gives it the whole 30): either way a retried failure, in the same words. A request that got no answer at all -- refused, reset, a name that does not resolve -- xfx also says it retries, where fx asks again only after its deadline, a reset or a closing connection (`isRetryableGatewayError`, `src/gateway/client.zig:19-23@481104fd`) and says `Unable to load models.` for the rest, a refused connection among them: MODELS-REASK's choice, unchanged here. **The llmux daemon's catalog has bounds of xfx's own** -- 3 s to connect, 5 s in all, 1 MiB, read the same way, whole or not at all -- because fx has no llmux and the daemon is on this machine; an answer past 1 MiB or not UTF-8 is a malformed catalog (`check the daemon is an llmux version compatible with xfx`), and one that never came whole -- no connection, a failed status (404, 503), a body cut short -- is an unavailable one (`check the daemon is running`); `xfx setup llmux`'s two probes read the same way. parsed for upstream's fields (language rows only; id, vendor, release, `tool-use`, efforts, context window, output ceiling, and Fast/Ultra from `fast_options` or the priced service tiers). A Gateway with no credential has no catalog: upstream falls back to the public one, and xfx does not contact a remote host it was given no key for. With one word it is upstream's router form: the runtime resolves it against the catalog -- loading it first when none is loaded -- an id or an alias spelled exactly, else upstream's fuzzy score (substring, every word, letters in order, any word) over the ids in menu order -- the router's own, which v0.0.16 kept as c1db919 had it (`fuzzyModelScore` and `resolveModelQueryFromIds`, `session_commands.zig:1425-1448,1760@481104fd`, unchanged) while the menu and the column moved to the ranks above -- else the word as typed, **which is applied rather than refused**, as upstream applies an id its catalog does not list. When the band holds the catalog it resolves the word itself, by the same rule (`provider::model::resolve_in_order`), and sends the id -- so the change is announced the moment it is given, as upstream's is; with no catalog in hand the word goes as typed and the runtime's answer is the announcement. **A resolved id is held to the typed word's rule** (`provider::model::model_id_problem`: one word, at most 200 bytes, no control character, C1 included): a catalog id that breaks it is refused, not adopted -- by the runtime, and by the band before it announces an id it resolved or a row it picked -- and the refusal is xfx's own words, never the id. So is every model the profile holds: a settings `model` or `models.<provider>` that breaks the rule is dropped at load with the `invalid_value` diagnostic, and no writer puts one there (`/model`, a speed save, `xfx setup` and `/setup`, `/provider`), `xfx setup llmux` passing over a catalog entry whose name breaks it and refusing a catalog with none that keeps it. Upstream adopts a resolved id (`setResolvedModelRuntime`, `session_commands.zig:1256-1260` at `c1db9191`, `:1399` at `481104fd`) and refuses only the writes that would persist it -- the session's (`validateModel`, `session_codec.zig:3165` at `c1db9191`, `:3830` at `481104fd`) and the settings' (`validateUserPatch`, `putModelPreference`, `settings_store.zig:921,1633`) -- and refuses such a model when it reads settings (`config_runtime.zig:1645,1674`); xfx refuses it at adoption as well, which is unsafe to copy, because the catalog is the provider's document, its parser takes any non-empty id, and an id adopted is announced and painted at once. **The inline column** (`input_completion_runtime.zig:1123-1295`): `/model ` and a space open the catalog's models under the argument -- from the column the argument starts at, six rows at most, the marked row in the bold hint grey and the rest dim -- and the text after it searches them as the menu does, the same ranks in the catalog's order and the first 32 of them (`modelCompletions`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:895-913@481104fd`), so `/model gpt6` lists `openai/gpt-6-astra`, the mark starting on the first ranked row -- and Tab, which writes the marked row, puts it back there, as fx's replacement does (`composer_text_replacement.zig:73-92`), since the line written can list fuzzy matches under its own row; a space typed after a query that is not a model's exact id is not inserted, as in fx (the Space branch, `app_input_runtime.zig:1355-1363@481104fd`, inserts nothing when `advanceModelPickerOnSpace`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:1210-1219`, finds no exact id), so a query of several words reaches the column pasted; Up and Down (and `C-k`/`C-j`) wrap, PgUp and PgDn move a page of the composer's row cap -- in every column, a page past either end landing on the other end (`navigateModelPicker`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:1072-1096@481104fd`; `fx-p5-page-keys-column-24x120-model-pgdn`, `-effort-pgup`, `-speed-pgdn`) -- Tab writes the marked row into the composer, Escape puts the column away until the line stops being a `/model` line, and Enter on a model with reasoning levels writes `/model <id> ` and opens the **effort column** under the next token: `default`, then the catalog's levels in its order, filtered by what is typed, Enter taking the level spelled out or the marked one -- and, for a model whose row offers Fast, writing `/model <id> <level> ` and opening the **speed column** under the next token (`normal`, then `fast` and `ultrafast` where the row offers them, `fast` marked), whose Enter makes the change; for a model without Fast the level's Enter makes the change, keeping the session's Fast only as far as the row offers it -- so a model that offers Ultra and not Fast cannot be given Ultra from the column, as upstream's cannot (`submitModelPicker`, `:1183`). fx v0.0.16 opens the same effort column on its own for an `/effort ` line, over the model in force and with no column before it (the `/effort` row). A model with no levels and a speed opens the speed column at once (`/model <id> auto `); one with neither is applied from the model column at once. The speed column marks a speed rather than a row (a typed speed resets it to `normal`), Tab writes all three tokens, Left goes back to the levels with the session's own speed marked, and a level spelled out and a space opens it for a model with either speed. Left steps back a column. A space at the column is the column's: after an id spelled exactly it is Enter -- so a typed `/model <id> <more>` for a model with no levels and no speed makes the change and sends the rest as a prompt, as upstream's does -- after a level spelled exactly it opens the speed column for a model with a speed, and otherwise it does nothing. Typing or a Backspace at the end of the effort column keeps it; any other edit, a paste included, goes back to the models. Without rows the column says `loading models...` (and asks the runtime for them, as a control message, whatever the queue holds), `unable to load models` -- and the next frame that draws it asks again once the failure's hold is over, as upstream's render path lists the column's models through `ensureModelCache` (`app_render_runtime.zig:559-568@481104fd`, `src/main.zig:2167-2169`), saying `unable to load models` still while that read is on its way, as upstream's column reads the cache's state, which a retry leaves `.failed` (`app_render_runtime.zig:752-753`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:633-634`), so the column turns into the rows when a read succeeds; an idle session paints no frame and asks nothing, as upstream's does -- `no matching models`, `no matching effort` or `no matching mode`; while a model is pending the hint row previews it, the marked level and the marked speed -- `⚡︎` for Fast from the moment the levels open on a model that offers it, as upstream's does. A row too long for the column's room ends in `…` here, where fx cuts it at the screen's edge and adds nothing (`composePickerOptionRowAnnotated` writes the label with `row_text.appendClipped`, `picker_presentation.zig:849@481104fd`, `row_text.zig:23-53`; `fx-p5-page-keys-anchor-24x60-model-wrapped`, the ledger's `model-column-clip`). Owner: MODEL-COLUMN-CLIP. Enter on a menu row goes on into the effort column for a model with levels (`beginExactModelSelection`) and applies one without. **The explicit form** `/model <id> <effort>`: a literal id, never fuzzy-resolved, and an effort the catalog lists for it or `auto`/`default`; it is applied with that effort and announced; a third word is the speed -- `fast` or `ultrafast` where the row offers it, `normal` where it offers either to decline -- and two words mean `normal`; anything else -- four words, an unlisted effort, a speed the row does not offer -- is upstream's `✗ usage: /model <id> <effort> [normal|fast|ultrafast]`, with the composer kept. **The reasoning effort** is one per session, carried across model changes, saved as `effort` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` (profile-only, `auto` for the default) beside `models.<provider>` in the same write, and -- by the TUI; `xfx ask` and the line-oriented shell read the key but send no effort yet -- sent with every request to a model whose catalog row lists it, the catalog read before the first such turn when nothing has read it: the Gateway's v4 route as a top-level `"reasoning"` (`max` sent as `xhigh`, the route's top tier), llmux as `output_config.effort`, the field every llmux route reads it from; a model the catalog does not describe is sent none. **The speed** -- Fast and Ultra, exclusive, both off whenever the model changes by a query, and held to what the row offers by a pick -- is saved as `fast_mode`, `ultrafast_mode` and `fast_mode_model_bound` (profile-only) beside every model and speed change, and sent by the TUI on the Gateway as upstream's `providerOptions`: `{"gateway":{"speed":"fast"}}` for Fast on a row that offers it, `{"gateway":{"only":["openai"]},"openai":{"serviceTier":"ultrafast"}}` for Ultra -- which, with no silent downgrade, refuses the turn unless the provider is the Gateway, the id is `openai/…` and the row offers it. **llmux reads no per-request speed** (its Fast is the daemon's own codex setting, `2lab-ai/llmux src/config/schema.rs:545-550`, and its catalog publishes no speed), so no llmux row offers one, the speed column never opens there, and nothing is sent. The announcement is upstream's `* Switched to <id> (effort: <level or default>, speed: <normal|fast|ultrafast>)` -- `fast` also for an id ending `-fast` -- or `* Next turn will use …` while a turn runs or waits; the hint row's compact label follows the model in force, with the level after it when the model takes it, then `⚡︎` -- in the row's grey for Fast (bound to the model, or an id ending `-fast`), in upstream's yellow (`38;2;255;204;0`, `38;5;220` at 256 colours) for Ultra. **A change is a control message**, never refused for a full queue: while a turn runs -- or a permission or model question waits, which it does not answer -- it is put aside and applied the moment that turn ends, before anything queued behind it. One put aside while `/provider` switched is saved for the provider it was chosen under and lands on no selector of the new one (upstream refuses the switch while work runs, so its end state is the same), with a warning that says so. A failed catalog load says upstream's `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` (or `AI Gateway rate limited model discovery; xfx retries automatically.`) where asking again could help -- upstream's words since 14a12fea, its own name read as xfx's -- and `Unable to load models.` otherwise (`retryableFailureText`, `model_menu_presentation.zig:435-443@481104fd`); either is read again past its hold. Upstream's third retryable sentence, `Could not refresh model catalog; xfx retries automatically.` in xfx's name, is for any other failure asking again could change: a catalog read that could be given no thread, or no runtime to run on (upstream's `.runtime`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:537-546`), told the moment it is found, a turn running or not, and read again past its hold like any other. A read past its deadline is upstream's retryable `.transport` (`catalogRequestFailure`, `builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2519`, its test `:2545-2547`) -- `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` -- which xfx says for a read whose send times out, or whose body is still coming at the deadline (`GatewayCatalog::fetch`, CATALOG-BOUNDS). The choice is saved as `models.<provider>` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` -- with `provider`, the speed keys and, for a pick (a menu row, the column, the explicit form, a `C-p` row, `/settings`' model list) of a model whose row lists levels, `effort` -- the one it chose or the session's -- as upstream's `selectModelFromPicker` writes them (`session_commands.zig:886-915`; a query writes no effort, `setResolvedModel`, `:1172-1188`) -- on the runtime thread, through the profile's staged `0600` writer, every other key kept, and read back at the next launch under the usual precedence (`XFX_MODEL` over the profile over the compiled default); when a higher layer will still decide a fresh session's model it says `* model: saved to user settings (scope=user); fresh sessions here use higher-precedence model=<layer>` -- `model picker:` for a change made in a column, the menu or the explicit form -- and a failed write `✗ model: active for this process but not saved to user settings (…)`. Every settings write -- a preference, and `xfx setup`'s, which merges the selection it decided into the file as it stands under the lock -- is one read-modify-write under an in-process lock and upstream's cross-process `settings.lock` beside the file, so `/statusline`, `/model` and a setup writing at once all survive. A durable `preferences_changed` event records the model, and the effort and both switches with every change to them -- and at the session's first save, which is the first prompt or a `/rename` before it, the ones in force, right behind `session_started`: a change made before then had no log to go into, and upstream holds every setting made before the first turn and writes them with it (`hold`, `writeFirstTurn`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:415-429`, `:503-539@481104fd`) -- and a resume -- at launch or from the picker -- puts the session's effort and speed back, as upstream's `restoreRuntimePreferences` does. **The line-oriented shell** keeps its own report: bare, the model, the provider and the layer that chose it, and the provider's catalog in the order it was published, bounded at 100 rows with an explicit `... and N more` line; with an argument the same resolution against a catalog it has loaded (an id it does not list is applied, not refused), the shared caveat (`provider::model::unverified_notice`) when no catalog could be consulted, and no profile write. **What a model id may be is one question with one answer on both surfaces**: `provider::model::model_id_problem` -- one printable word of at most 200 bytes, because the id becomes an HTTP header and a session-log field -- asked before the runtime is told anything. Both run the same decision procedure and write the same bytes as the `setup` subcommand, because all three are `provider::setup::prepare` followed by `provider::setup::commit` -- one decision procedure, one writer. **That shared pair is all the `setup` subcommand and the line shell perform**: they prepare, commit and re-read the configuration, and they do **not** run the snapshot/classify/rollback transaction described next, which is the TUI worker's alone. In the TUI the whole of it is a transaction on the runtime thread: the profile's preimage is snapshotted first, every network and read step runs before anything is written, a cancellation may win **only** before the commit, a commit error is classified by rereading the raw bytes rather than by trusting the error value, the configuration is then re-read from the file that was written, and a reload failure rolls back only while the disk still holds exactly the document this transaction wrote -- bytes and Unix mode both. Bytes it did not write are never overwritten: a third state on disk means another writer owns the file, and the session ends rather than clobbering it. What the shell shows afterwards -- the model, the credential fact -- is what the reload resolved to rather than what the write intended, so a settings layer that outranks the profile is named instead of silently winning. A switch drops the bundle -- a connection to an endpoint no longer configured -- and **keeps the conversation**, as upstream's `/provider` keeps its session (`app_auth_runtime.zig:1377-1381`): its log, grants, read proofs, `/undo` journal, title and the meter's tokens go on; the switch is written into the log as `preferences_changed` with the provider and the model (`commitRuntimePreferences`, `app_session_runtime.zig:3539-3550`); and the new provider's catalog is read at once, in the background as at launch (the `/model` row), as upstream's switch adopts the target's (`:1377`), so the meter measures the same tokens against the new window. The next request carries every earlier turn in the new provider's wire, rebuilt from the log: text, tool calls, results (a refusal as `execution-denied` on the Gateway and `is_error` on llmux), images, steers and a compaction checkpoint all translate. A step's sealed reasoning -- llmux's signed `thinking` blocks -- goes only to the provider **and the model** that sealed it, so a `/model` change on llmux drops it too, and the rest of the step goes as it is: its text and its calls (upstream's `ProviderReplay.matches` and `projectProviderReplay`, `types.zig:1512-1515`, `:1548-1563`); upstream drops it without a word, xfx says so once per conversation (`! session: this session recorded reasoning on the anthropic_messages wire and is continuing on vercel_gateway, so that reasoning was not carried over` after a switch, `! session: this session recorded reasoning from <model> and is continuing with <model>, so that reasoning was not carried over` after a model change). **It is said once it is handed to the screen:** a turn stopped before its request -- in its catalog wait (the `/model` row) -- hands nothing on and sends nothing, so the next turn, which sends the history, says it. The screen paints every notice it is handed, a stopped turn's too; a reset and a resume's or a fork's replay drop one, and with it the conversation whose ledger marked it, so a later conversation of the same session says it again. Upstream's lines said once a session are its `[context]` notices (`claimContextNotice`, `session.zig:1551-1561@481104fd`), each claimed where it is said: a project file's at submission, written to the transcript in the same step (`refreshProjectContext`, `app_agent_runtime.zig:906-938`, from `main.zig:1200`), which `processQueuedPrompt` then finds said (`:976-996`); the skill catalog's and the MCP catalog's only once the turn is past its stop checks (`orchestrator.zig:5950`, `:6001`, after `:5893-5948`; `app_callbacks.zig:1294-1307`, `app_agent_runtime.zig:852-867`), and claimed when they are queued rather than painted; skill diagnostics at launch and by `/skills`, written in the same step (`app_bootstrap_runtime.zig:292-308`, `app_commands.zig:1083-1098`). A read's rule is applied on both. Every answer is under `mcp`, usage included: a verb's output one neutral notice and its errors one warning (`VerbJob.run`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:585-602`), and a change reloads in place and says nothing (`MCP client runtime` row). `p5-mcp-empty` and `p5-mcp-verbs` against fx v0.0.16 (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/mcp-verbs/`). **In typed order, where fx's is the order of completion**: fx runs each typed `/mcp` on a detached thread and reports it when it ends (`startVerbs`, `VerbJob`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:551-604`), so in `p5-mcp-verbs` its `show remote`, waiting on a connect, was printed after the `show proj` and `login fixture` typed after it; that is a race no receipt can pin, and xfx answers on the MCP runtime's one worker thread in the order typed -- so a `show` that waits holds the lines typed after it, and the menu's reads, for that wait. A word, a flag or a URL typed is said back spelled by `terminal_safe`, where fx says it as typed, and only as far as its first `=` (`/mcp add has no --env= flag.`, where fx says `--env=TOKEN=…` whole) -- a server's name too, which a hand-written profile can give one; a `--env` or `--header` value is never said back. **Refused before the write, where fx writes it**: `add --project --header Authorization=…` is `Couldn't add X to .mcp.json: xfx won't send an Authorization header from .mcp.json; add the server without --project to keep it in ~/.xfx/mcp.json.` -- xfx's reader skips such an entry (MCP project servers row), so the shared file would hold a token xfx never sends -- matched ignoring case, as the reader matches it. **And nothing the reader would skip**: the entry `add` would write is put to the reader's own rules first (`config::unreadable`, `parse_entry` over the writer's JSON), so a header name with a blank in it (` Authorization=…` included), a reserved one, a control in a value, or a URL the reader won't connect to (plain `http` off loopback, credentials, a fragment) is `Couldn't add X to FILE: its "headers" isn't valid.` / `its "url" isn't valid.` with nothing written, where fx writes it and says `Added`; a `${…}` in a project's URL is left for the expansion an approval brings, after what no expansion changes is checked (its syntax, the scheme, and no literal `@` or `#` anywhere after `://`, since where the authority ends is the expansion's to say -- **residual**: an `@` in a templated path is refused too, where fx writes it), and a profile's URL, which nothing expands, is checked as written. A choice `approve` or `reject` can't save says xfx's `Project MCP choices were not applied: …`, where fx says `Couldn't save that choice: …`. **`/mcp ` completes its argument** as fx's does (`verb_completions`, `buildCompletion`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:459-515`; `src/tui/mcp_args.rs`): the eight verbs with what each does, then `show` and `remove NAME` for each server whose name a command can carry, `login` and `logout` for a remote one, `approve` and `reject` for a project one, its status beside it, read twice a second; under the argument, no header, six rows, the hint row left to the status line (`p5-mcp-verbs` `complete-args`, `complete-show`). Up, Down, `C-j` and `C-k` move the mark, PgUp and PgDn a page of the composer's row cap, from past either end to the other end (fx's slash list, `navigateSlashCompletion(picker_delta)`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:255-260@481104fd`; `fx-p5-page-keys-mcp-24x120-args-pgdn`, `-pgup`) -- the arrows and the pages only while the composer is one row, the caret's on a taller one (`-wrapped-up`, `-wrapped-pgup`) -- Tab writes the marked line, Escape closes it for the line, and **Enter submits the marked line in place of what was typed** (`resolveSlashSubmission`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:1819-1824@481104fd`), so `/mcp remove web` with only `web2` configured removes `web2`, as fx does in `p5-mcp-verbs`. A read that drops a line above the mark keeps the mark on its line, not its index, and one that drops the marked line closes the list until the text changes, so Enter submits what was typed; and a `/mcp` line the history walk hands back opens none until it is edited, so Up goes on walking (`history_slash_recall_until_edit`, `picker_state.zig:451-466`, as a recalled slash word is). A workspace's `.mcp.json` servers wait for the person, unasked: fx v0.0.16 has no launch prompt (`ba0ea4a9`; c1db9191's raw two-row prompt, `main.zig:1818-1832@c1db9191`, its `1`/`2`/`3` and its Escape dismissal are gone), and the status line says `X is waiting for approval · /mcp` once the servers settle (MCP client runtime row). Nothing starts one before it is approved -- not the launch, a reload, or opening the menu, whose tool list for a held server is fx's reason for holding it (`heldReason`, `src/core/mcp_host/runtime.zig:2038-2053@481104fd`), never a start. **The approval's screen** is fx's (`proj  waiting for approval`, `Approve X for this project? It runs:` or `It connects to:`, then what it runs or reaches wrapped at spaces and never cut, `enter confirm     esc cancel`; presentation `:437-499`, `:507-515`), spelled before it is wrapped, so a control spelled out wraps as the cells it takes. **Where xfx's differs -- deliberately, because upstream's is unsafe to copy**: (1) it shows what the child is given, from the entry the runtime read (`ShownEntry`: its rows and the digest an approval binds, from one read) -- the command line, each word as a POSIX shell would read it back -- a word xfx expands (`${NAME}`, `${NAME:-default}`) in double quotes, every other `$`, `"`, `\` and `` ` `` escaped, so the reader expands what xfx will, and a control character beside an expansion an ANSI-C piece between quoted ones (`"a"$'\u001B'"${X}"`; one inside a default, which has no exact spelling there, stays in the quotes as the row spells it, `\x1b`) -- or the address; then one row `env: NAME=value` for each variable a stdio entry sets, or `header: Name: value` for each header a remote entry sends (a `header_env` header as `X-Team: ${TEAM}`, `bearer_token_env` as `Authorization: Bearer ${TOKEN}`, as they are sent) -- every value as written, since a pending entry is never expanded: a literal, a default with its text (`${NOPE:-./y}`), a `${VAR}` as the name it is, and never a value from xfx's environment. fx's shows the command line or the address alone (`confirmLines`, presentation `:442-481`). xfx's rule since 2026-11-04 (`c4d252f5`): a variable changes what runs as much as the command does -- `"PATH": "./x:${PATH}"` makes `node` run `./x/node`, since the command is resolved with the child's PATH, and `NODE_OPTIONS`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_*` or `PYTHONPATH` change it as much -- so a key alone hides what is approved. The entry is the repository's own file, so its text shows nothing the file does not; a credential written into it literally (by a person, or by `/mcp add --project --env`) is shown as the file shows it, and one in xfx's environment never is. Values a person typed are still never said back by the verbs (`typed`), and a profile's are never shown by `list` or `show`: neither is an approval. A server's command line and address -- in `show`, on the menu's screen, and in `approve`'s sentence -- are its file's, `${VAR}` and all, a profile server's and an approved project server's alike: what an expansion puts in is for the server (the profile and project rows; `ServerConfig::shown_command`, `shown_url`), and reaches a screen only if the server prints it to its stderr (the client runtime row). Every row is spelled as the approval view spells a command it asks about (`approval_view::command_text`: every control `\xNN`, a line break included, and each bidirectional, zero-width or invisible code point the table names `\u{NNNN}`, so a `U+202E` cannot draw the command backwards); a row longer than 1024 bytes, which would fill the screen, is its first 512 and last 256 bytes with its length and where it is whole between them (`[N bytes in all; .mcp.json has every byte]`), never cut without saying so -- and such an entry is not shown whole, so Enter approves nothing (`A row of X's entry is too long to show here whole, so nothing was approved: .mcp.json has every byte, and /mcp approve X approves the entry as xfx read it.`). A row the width wraps goes on in rows led by `↳ `, two columns in, where each row of the entry's own is four in -- every wrapped piece, of a command line or address alone too, so none passes for a row the entry has (`v`, a run of spaces, `env: PATH=/usr/bin`; an address `https://a.example/mcp?q=`, spaces, `header: X-Team: blue`, opus-B). The cost: fx's goes on four columns in, so a wrapped command line or address here is led by `↳ ` where fx's is not. (2) It takes the rows it needs past fx's thirteen, up to the space the screen gives a panel, and Enter approves only what a frame drew whole -- every row in the panel's rows and none cut with `…`, as the row measures it, a cluster at a time: one that could not, or that something wrote over since (a refused write, a resize, a screen the band is blind on), approves nothing and says `xfx couldn't show all of what X runs on this screen, so nothing was approved.`. (3) Enter approves the entry that frame drew, by its digest (`TrustAction::ApproveShown`), so what is saved is the entry xfx read and showed, and a reload admits it only while the file still holds it -- a server a reload keeps keeps its screen's entry only while that entry's digest is the fresh read's, so an entry respelled (`"type": "stdio"` added) is shown, and approved, as it now is: a rewrite read before Enter approves nothing (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx showed it; nothing was approved.`, and the screen shows the new one next), and one on disk not yet read is saved as the entry shown, which the next read finds changed and leaves waiting. fx's Approve runs `approve NAME`, which approves what its host holds when the verb runs (`verbs.zig:631-646`). An approval is for that entry, not for its name. Upstream keys it by name alone (`resolveAdmission`, `src/core/mcp/project_config.zig:825-832@c1db9191`, unchanged at `:938-945@481104fd`) while its auto mode writes `.mcp.json` without review like any other workspace file (`src/core/tooling/tool_admission.zig:663-693@c1db9191`, `:662-692@481104fd`): a model's file write could swap an approved server's command or address and have it run on the next reload, unasked. In xfx a changed entry is pending again (MCP project servers and trust, below) and waits for the menu or `approve`. Nothing reloads the MCP configuration without the person -- launch, and an `add`, `remove`, `approve` or `reject`, typed or chosen in the menu, are the only triggers; there is no file watch and no model tool that reloads. The typed `approve --all` keeps fx's behaviour: every entry the last read showed as a pending server, not ones added later (`CHANGELOG.md:165@481104fd`), and not one that parses only once expanded (`"type": "${T:-stdio}"` is shown as a configuration error, not as a server); `approve NAME`, typed, is the one way to approve such an entry: it has no server to find by name, so `approve` approves the entry the runtime last read, bound to that read's digest as the parsed path is, and only while `.mcp.json` still holds it (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx read it; nothing was approved.` otherwise, and fx's `No project server named …` for an entry the runtime never read), saying what it runs or reaches but never its `env` or `headers` -- fx's `approve` finds only servers that parse (`verbs.zig:631-646@481104fd`), so this is xfx's, kept from c1db9191's `trust approve <name>`; `reject NAME` reaches such an entry the same way. An approval is for the entry, not for the workspace code it loads: `node server.js` is approved as that command line, and an edit to `server.js` asks nothing (a later unit looks at that). What a server or a repository supplied -- the menu's names, addresses, command lines, errors, stderr tails, tool names and descriptions -- is spelled as fx's footer spells it (`encodeTerminalSafeInline(.., 4096)`, `row_text.zig:127-134@481104fd`, through `terminal_safe`: controls, C1, zero-width, bidirectional and byte-order-mark code points, and the other default-ignorable ones the approval view names (SEC-A4), spelled out rather than dropped) and cut to its row, where fx writes such a byte as `?` or spells it; an approval's rows are spelled as the approval view spells a command (above), and cut only as above. A server's name is said only as far as its first `=`, as the verbs say it (`typed`): a value after one may be a secret. **Gated, not built** (signing in opens a browser and stores a token, which needs its own decision -- zbrain open-loop #88 covers Codex and Grok only): `login NAME [--no-browser]` makes fx's checks first -- the server is there (`No MCP server named X. /mcp list shows them.`), it is remote (`X runs a command, so it has no login.`), nothing holds it back (`X is waiting for approval: /mcp approve X.`, `X is disabled.`; `login`, `verbs.zig:765-806@481104fd`) -- and then says `Interactive MCP authentication is unavailable here.` (`mcp.zig:154-160@c1db9191`) where fx looks for how to sign in: `Couldn't sign in to remote: fx couldn't find how to sign in to it.` in `p5-mcp-verbs` comes from a discovery xfx never starts. `logout` of a remote server is fx's `X wasn't signed in.` (`verbs.zig:689-716`), true of every server since xfx keeps no login, and `remove` never says fx's `, and deleted its login`. A bare `add slack` (fx's Slack server, to sign in to) says `Interactive MCP authentication is unavailable here.` and writes nothing; `add slack URL` is an ordinary add, as fx's is. **Narrower than upstream's**: a settings write keeps no `backups/` copy, as no xfx settings write does. **The line shell** says the TUI is where MCP runs, and starts no server. |
-| `/skills` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/skills [list|add|install|show|create|remove|path] [name|url|path]`, "browse and manage skills", Extensions, with an argument (`commands.zig:483`; grammar `src/builtins/skills.zig:93-104`, `parseCommand`). **Where skills come from** is upstream's discovery (`skill_runtime.zig:336-1046`) over **xfx's own roots only**: `.xfx/skills` then `skills/` in the workspace and each ancestor short of the home directory, then the managed root `~/.xfx/skills`; candidates in byte order, a directory holding a regular `SKILL.md` whose frontmatter is upstream's (`name`, optional `description` inline or as a `>`, `>-` or `|` block, 64 KiB header cap -- `skill_contract.zig`), a link followed only inside its workspace ancestor or a directory the profile's `skill_symlink_authorities` or `XFX_SKILL_SYMLINK_AUTHORITIES` names, and never in the managed root (below), the same directory counted once, and every candidate skipped said as `! skills: skill discovery warning: …` before the answer. **The decided default:** upstream also reads other agents' roots (`.opencode/`, `.codex/`, `.claude/`, `.agents/`, `.claw/skills` in the workspace and at home, `src/builtins/skills.zig:16-38`); xfx does not, because on a machine with another agent installed it would put that agent's instructions into xfx's prompt. **The list the TUI reads** is discovered on a worker thread -- first at launch, so the first frame does not wait for it, then again on request -- and held between refreshes (`beginStartupSkillsLoad`, `app_bootstrap_runtime.zig:278-289`; `CatalogRefreshTask`, `requestRefresh`, `pollRefresh`, `skill_runtime.zig:2181-2310`); the `$` menu, the slash popup, the ghost after the caret and `/skills` all read it. One action waits for a refresh and a newer one takes its place (`queueRefreshAction`, `:2353-2383`): the launch's own, which says what discovery skipped once the first list lands; `list` and `show`, which open the menu when the refresh they asked for lands; and the sentence of a `create`, `remove` or install, said once the list holding the change lands -- `Installing from …` and every refusal at once (`queueSkillsNoticeAfterRefresh`, `app_commands.zig:1903-1951`). A refresh that fails says `* skills: Skills could not be refreshed. The previous catalog was not shown as current.` (at launch: `Skills could not be loaded. Run /skills to try again.`). There is no loading row: upstream's menu has none, only `No skills available.` and `No skills found.` (`composeEmptyRow`, `skills_menu_presentation.zig:411-431`). **The TUI**, bare or `list`: upstream's menu in the band under the composer, which stays and is its search -- `Skills N` and the source tabs `All`, `xfx` and `Workspace` (upstream's tabs for the families xfx reads; its `Claude`, `Codex`, `Agents`, `OpenCode` and `Claw` tabs are absent, there being no such skills to list), a blank row, one row per skill with its name and, from the widest name plus four, its scope (`xfx · Workspace`, `xfx · Global`), the marked row bold and the rest dim (`skills_menu_presentation.zig`); managed installs first, then workspace skills, a search ranking name prefix, then name, then description, source and path (`SkillMenuView`). Up/Down and `C-k`/`C-j` move and stop at either end, PgUp/PgDn page, Tab and Shift-Tab change the source, Enter puts the marked skill's **token** in the composer in place of the search, and Escape closes it and empties the search; the hint row says `↑↓ navigate     tab source     enter use     esc close`. `show <name>` opens it on the skill's own tab with it marked, or with the name as the search when two skills share it; `* skills: Skill 'nope' not found.` otherwise. **A `$`** typed outside an `@` path opens the same menu on the word after it, shown while some skill matches that word (`app_input_runtime.zig:1629-1641`, `menuVisible`); a space ends it, and Enter makes the word a token. A `$word` that arrives in a paste -- text, or beside a pasted image -- and names a skill opens it too (`maybeOpenPastedSkillMenu`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:612-663`). **The slash popup** lists the skills its query finds after its commands, in the menu's `All` order, each by its name with no slash, its description and its short source (`workspace .xfx`, `workspace skills/`, `global .xfx`), under `Results N` once a skill is among the rows (`prepareSlashMenu`, `slashMenuRowContent`, `composeSlashMenuHeaderRow`, `picker_presentation.zig:1100-1266`); Tab or Enter on a skill row makes the slash word its token, and Enter runs a command instead when the query is one's exact name (`bindSelectedSlashSkill`, `submitSlashPickerSelection`, `app_input_runtime.zig:2810-2919`). **The ghost after the caret** (`visibleInlineCompletion`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:598-640`): a `$word` ending the draft with no menu showing -- after the space that ends the `$` menu is taken back, say, or on a recalled line -- has the rest of the first skill name it begins (discovery order, ASCII case aside, `firstSkillNameCompletion`) painted after it in the dim grey and closed by a reset (`\e[38;5;245m…\e[0m`, `appendInlineCompletionSuffix`, `input_presentation.zig:955-973`), cut to the cells the row has and not shown when its first character does not fit; a slash word ending a draft that has other words before it has the rest of the first command it begins, in registry order, a name before its aliases (`firstSlashCompletion`, `command_specs.zig:490-506`). A draft that is one slash word is the popup's and has none. Right with nothing selected and Tab take it -- a skill's as its token in place of the `$word`, a command's as the letters with no space -- and Enter sends the draft as typed. Escape dismisses it until the draft stops being that kind of word, as Escape on the `$` menu does its word's; with a turn running, Escape arms the interrupt over a skill's instead. None shows under a question or while `/help`, `/settings`, `/model`'s list, the session picker or a skills menu owns the band, and a recalled slash word shows none until it is edited (`inlineCompletionSurfaceAvailable`, `:703-727`; `picker_state.zig:142-167`, `:451-467`). A token is the skill's **name**, painted bold in the composer and in the card's accent on the card, a space after it when the next character would join a word; it is one unit (a delete over it takes it whole), an edit that takes one or puts one in is an undo boundary as an image badge's is, a kill and a yank carry it, and a recalled line brings it back. The prompt carries it as `$name` (`"text":"$demo run it"`), and the turn **binds** the exact directory the menu chose. **What the model is told**, on every request of every front end (TUI, line shell, `ask`) from the skills discovered as the turn starts: upstream's `<available_skills>` system block before the project instructions, byte for byte -- two sentences, one `Root N: <dir>` per directory, one `- <name>: <description> (location: skill:<namespace>:<root>/<leaf>)` per skill, the namespace upstream's SHA-256 of the advertised names and paths, values written as upstream's model scalars, descriptions cut at 1024 characters, the block kept to upstream's budget -- two percent of the model's context window at four bytes a token (a 200k window is 16000 bytes), from the catalog the TUI reads at launch or, in the line shell and `ask`, from the window the Gateway is known to give the model; 8000 characters when no window is known (`SkillPromptBudget.resolve`, `skill_runtime.zig:3061-3083`) -- by shortening descriptions and then omitting entries from the end with `Omitted skills: N.` (`renderSkillPrompt`, `:3115-3322`) -- and, when the prompt names skills (a bound token, a `$name` outside quotes, code and `@` paths, a leading `/name`, or "use the X skill", not after a negation -- `collectExplicitSkillSelections`, `:2696-2797`), upstream's `Explicitly invoked skill content for this query:` block after the project instructions with each skill's `SKILL.md` whole in `<skill_content name=… location=… resource="SKILL.md" complete="true">` (`buildExplicitPromptSection`, `skill_invocation.zig:205-289`), on that turn's requests only. The TUI says `* 1 requested skill loaded` / `└ Loaded skill demo` under the card, or `! Requested skills · 0 loaded · 1 failed (ctrl+o for details)` with each failure on the full transcript; the line shell says the same rows as `[shell]` lines. A prompt with a token is not steered into a running turn, as upstream's is not (`sameTurnSteeringEligible`). **`path`**: `* skills: xfx workspace roots are auto-discovered from .xfx/skills and skills/.` and `xfx managed install root: <home>/.xfx/skills` -- upstream's third line, about compatibility roots, is not said, xfx reading none. **`create <name>`** writes upstream's template `SKILL.md` into the managed root (`* skills: Created <path>`), a name with `/` or `\` refused in upstream's words. **`remove <name>`** deletes only a managed skill (`* skills: Removed skill 'made'.`); any other is refused naming where it is (`Skill 'demo' comes from workspace .xfx/skills, not the xfx managed install root. By default none is named, so a link out of the workspace is refused with fx's diagnostic, and a link in the managed root is never followed. **Fail closed:** an unreadable list in any profile layer names no authority at all (`invalid_value key=skill_symlink_authorities skill_symlink_authorities must be an array of at most 32 absolute directory paths without .. components`), where upstream drops only the layer it could not parse. The authorities are read once, when the configuration is loaded at launch. **Narrower than upstream's**: a candidate's root is found missing through linked directories, where upstream's walk stops at a link. Not matched: switching collapse **off** expands only the open group -- a group written collapsed never had its calls on the screen; settings backups and shadowing suffixes. **The line-oriented shell** has no menu and says where it is. |

-| full-screen TUI | ui | partial | Opt-in and narrower than upstream's. `XFX_TUI=1` on a bare `xfx` takes the terminal into raw mode on its **normal** buffer: the band, the transcript and the composer live there for the life of the session and never move, and mouse reporting is never enabled, so the terminal's own scrollback and wheel keep working. The alternate screen is an owned excursion and nothing else -- `\x1b[?1049h` is written only to review a change too big for the band to show, for as long as that question is unanswered (below), and for the full transcript `C-o` opens, for as long as it is open (its own row below); a session that does neither, however long, writes it never. Under tmux the kitty keyboard push is omitted, because sending it there breaks key input. Raw mode is an obligation rather than a feature: the entry bits (`ECHO`/`ICANON`/`IEXTEN`/`ISIG` clear, `CS8`, `VMIN=1`, `VTIME=0`) are asserted positively while the child runs, and the `termios` captured before entry is put back on exit -- onto the descriptor it was captured from, and whether or not the screen could still be written. The band is a divider, the composer, and a hint row at the bottom of the screen -- with one more row above the divider while a turn is running, saying what it is doing, and a block of rows between the two while that turn is waiting to be told whether it may change something; every frame is one write wrapped in synchronized output (`?2026h`/`l`) -- a frame that also grows the document carries those rows inside the same update, ahead of the band, as upstream's frame carries its movement bytes (`composeWireFrame`, `src/ui/render_engine/terminal_diff.zig:604-645` at `c1db919`), so a terminal that honours the mode never presents a new row with the band still where the scroll left it -- and what that write carries is the **difference** between what the terminal is holding and what it should be holding: the band keeps a shadow grid of grapheme cells, wide-cell continuations and attribute state, builds the grid the frame would leave, and emits a `CUP`, replacement text and an `EL` for the rows that changed and for nothing else -- so a keystroke costs the cell it changed and an animation phase costs the digit it turned over. **A frame whose cells, window title and caret are all unchanged writes zero bytes**, which is what keeps an idle session silent between the loop's 8 ms ticks. The shadow is advanced only by a write that succeeded, so a refused frame is owed again rather than believed; a Ctrl-L and the resume after a `SIGTSTP` mark the screen externally damaged, and the frame after either opens with the Phase-1 erase -- the rows a shrinking band gave back, then `CUP` to the band's top row and `ED` -- because a forgotten shadow is *unknown* rather than blank, and a diff that treated it as blank would rewrite the band's own columns and leave whatever the shell put beside them on every band row. The erase starts at the band's top row and never above it, so the terminal's own document survives a resume. A resume also re-arms the window title: the restore that hands the terminal back pops the title stack, so the title the session set is gone, and the band owes a fresh `OSC 2` -- one push per time the terminal is taken and one pop per time it is given back, so a stop/resume cycle leaves the stack balanced. The optimization is judged by the screen it leaves rather than by the bytes it saves: the `fault-injection` build keeps the Phase-1 whole-band painter behind a compile-time seam (`Fault::FullPaintReference`, absent from every released binary), and `scripts/smoke-tui.sh`'s scenario 13 drives identical facts through both painters on a real pseudoterminal and requires the final cells, attributes and caret to be equal. **The terminal's window title is set** -- `OSC 2 ; xfx · <model>`, with every control stripped out of the model label so a configured name cannot close the sequence and have the rest of itself executed -- and it is borrowed rather than taken: the mode set pushes the terminal's own title onto its stack (`CSI 22 ; 2 t`) and every restore path, normal and abnormal, pops it back (`CSI 23 ; 2 t`). A title is written as its own `OSC 2` right ahead of the frame's synchronized update and never inside it, which is where upstream's lands: upstream writes a title the moment it changes, as a write of its own between frames (`syncTerminalTitleWith`, `src/core/app/app_session_runtime.zig:3608-3629`; `setTerminalTitleLabel`, `src/ui/render.zig:723-733` at `c1db919`), so its bytes reach the terminal after one frame's `?2026l` and before the next one's `?2026h`; the output check refuses an update led by anything else, or by a title other than the one the band owes. The line-oriented shell sets no title and pushes nothing. **What has already reached the terminal's own scrollback is never rewritten**: the diff writes only at or below the band's top row, plus the rows a shrinking band gave back, and a document append still scrolls the screen with real linefeeds -- committed rows stay the terminal's. A terminal smaller than 6 rows or 20 columns is refused with a reason rather than painted over. **What those rows say**: the divider spans the screen, the composer shows what has been typed into it behind its prompt marker, and the hint row says what a turn would be run with -- in upstream's order, joined by upstream's separator: a call to action when the configured provider has no usable credential (a Gateway with no bearer token or a llmux with no URL -- both refuse every turn, and the row says so before a prompt is spent on one), the permission mode, and a compact model label that drops the provider prefix and the `claude-` family prefix. A refusal takes the left of it whole when one has just happened, and `esc again to clear` sits flush with the last column while the double-Esc gesture is armed, the row's colours closed before its padding as upstream's are (`input_presentation.zig:551-567`). On a screen too narrow for all of it the segments are **dropped whole, from the right**, rather than clipped mid-number: the permission mode gives way in front of the model rather than the model behind it, and the right-hand warning is reserved before the left is measured, because it is about a key that is already armed. The meter upstream ends that row with is here too and is filled from a completed turn's own `input_tokens` over the context window the provider's catalog publishes, shown only when the session has both numbers and dropped whole rather than defaulted when it has one; what is still absent is the shortcut legend. **While a turn is in flight there is one row above the divider**, and only then: it says `• Thinking` with how long the turn has been running, or names the tool that is running instead of saying `Thinking` at all -- a turn that has gone quiet because a tool is taking a minute otherwise looks exactly like a turn that has gone quiet. The marker blinks every 500 ms, counted in the renderer's own 50 ms animation phases rather than on a second clock beside it, and the row asks for a frame when its text changes rather than on every phase, so a band whose row says the same thing is not repainted twenty times a second and an idle band asks for nothing at all. The row is the **band's** while it exists: it takes its row from the bottom of the document exactly as a composer that grew by one does, and the divider, the composer and the caret do not move when a turn starts. It is **exactly the turn's lifetime**: the runtime says when a turn begins and says when it ends, in order on the one channel that carries everything else it says, and the band draws the row between the two. Told rather than counted, and that is correctness rather than taste -- a prompt is accepted the moment it is typed and may then wait behind another turn for a minute (the hint row is where waiting is said, and a waiting prompt gets no row), and the place a finished turn holds is given back *after* its conclusion is sent, so a band counting places would announce a turn that had not started and start a clock for one that no longer exists. **The clock is one turn's**: a prompt queued behind a running one does not restart it, and when that turn ends the row begins again from zero for whatever turn the runtime starts next rather than carrying the finished one's elapsed time forward. A `/model` travels on the same channel but is not a turn, and no row appears for one. Two things named rather than implied: the elapsed clock **stops while xfx is waiting for a decision**, because that interval measures the person rather than the model -- the approval panel below is what pends one, and the row itself is off the band while a question is up, as upstream's is (`render_input.zig:705`), and comes back at the elapsed time it stopped at; and the token count upstream shows beside the elapsed time is **absent**, because the streamed event set carries no usage -- the row says nothing about tokens rather than reporting a zero nobody measured. What is *above* the divider is the turn, described below. The band's size is asked of **standard output**, because that is the screen it is drawn on; raw mode is entered on standard input, because that is where input arrives; and a redirected session whose two ends are different terminals gets no cursor report at all, so it starts at row 1 and pushes nothing. A screen that refuses every frame for half a second of wall-clock time ends the session with the first of those errors, on a terminal the exit still puts back, rather than being retried invisibly forever; the budget is measured on a clock rather than in repaints, because the 8 ms tick is the longest a turn waits and not the shortest. The exit clears from the band's top row downward (`app_lifecycle.zig:578-593`), and only when a band was really painted: a session that left before its first frame -- a Ctrl-D that arrived with the cursor report -- moves no cursor and erases nothing. At launch it asks the terminal where the cursor is (CSI `6n`, 100 ms deadline, row 1 if the terminal does not answer; the row under it when the cursor is past the first column) and pushes the shell output above that row into scrollback -- or, with `/settings`' `Startup scrollback` off, starts on that row under it and pushes only what leaves fewer than eleven document rows (`app_lifecycle.zig:944-988`) -- moving the cursor to the bottom row first, because a linefeed scrolls a terminal only from the bottom margin and from anywhere else merely walks the cursor down -- so the band never paints over what was already on screen; a keystroke typed while the query is in flight is deferred, not swallowed. Restoration is proven one case per exit path, each on a real pty: the normal exit, `SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP`/an external `SIGINT` (the child dies *by the signal*, which is what proves the handler reset the disposition and re-raised rather than fabricating a status) -- **except a `SIGINT` the process was started ignoring** (`nohup … &`), which stays ignored, so a `kill -INT` changes nothing and every command the session runs inherits the ignore, as upstream's TUI catches no `SIGINT` at all (`installAbnormalExitHandlers` owns `SIGTERM` and `SIGHUP` only, `app_lifecycle.zig:82-104` at `c1db9191`) -- and `SIGTSTP`/`SIGCONT` including a **second** `SIGTSTP` after a resume, which is what proves the stop handler was reinstalled. Every one of those is an ordinary transition rather than a race, because the owned signals are blocked across the whole of it -- from before the `tcsetattr` that takes the terminal until after the last handler is installed -- so a supervisor that signals xfx during startup finds a handler rather than a default disposition and a raw terminal. The signals named here are the ones a *supervisor or an operator* sends: with `ISIG` cleared a **typed** Ctrl-C or Ctrl-Z generates no signal at all, and arrives as a byte for the input decoder. Ctrl-C is a byte, not a signal, and it means what upstream's semantic Ctrl-C means (`app_input_runtime.zig:1675-1712` at `c1db919`): over a running turn with a draft in the composer it takes the draft and nothing else; otherwise every press is a press of one three-second exit window -- the first arms it, says `press ctrl+c again to exit` on the hint row for as long as it lasts (the `/help` catalog's row included), and stops the running turn or throws the idle draft away; a second inside it leaves with **130**, disjoint from the external `SIGINT` above, which still kills the process by the signal unless the launcher ignored it; any other keystroke closes the window early. A stopped turn leaves upstream's line, `▪ Cancelled · What can xfx do differently?`, at the keystroke: the answer stops where it was on the screen (the line it had not finished is not painted, nor is anything still crossing from the runtime) and no `✗ system:` or summary follows; a running command is killed at the keystroke with its process group, its row `└ Cancelled <command>` and its line `▪ Cancelled <command> · …` the turn's (the UI raises the turn's own flag, which the command's supervisor polls), where a running file call runs to its end first -- **and the interrupt drops whatever was queued behind it**, said as `⊘ queue: dropping what was queued behind it as well.`, because a queue that started running a moment after the user asked everything to stop would be the surprise the band's `┋` row is there to prevent; a prompt typed *after* the interrupt is a new intention and still runs, which the interrupt is told the boundary of rather than left to guess. What the model is told about the stopped turn is upstream's: the cut-off answer closed with `The previous response ended before completion.` (or the tools that finished, named), a call the turn stopped under -- one that never ran, a command the user stopped, or a question the user cancelled -- answered `aborted by user` as a failure, then the `<turn_aborted>` user message (`session.zig:52-64`, `:2695-2747`); any failed tool result is sent as a failure (`error-text` on the Gateway, `is_error` on llmux; `vercel_protocol.zig:742-747`), and a refusal as `execution-denied` with the refusal as its reason (`vercel_protocol.zig:742-744`; `is_error` on llmux, which has no word of its own). A typed Ctrl-Z still binds nothing -- upstream's suspends (`app_input_runtime.zig:862-866@481104fd`), so here only a `SIGTSTP` stops the session; owner unit: **CTRL-Z** (queued) -- and Ctrl-D is not an exit while a turn is running. Escape is a two-tap: over a running turn a second one within a second stops it, and the hint row says `esc again to interrupt` (narrower: `esc esc interrupt`, `esc esc`) in between; at an idle prompt with a draft a second one within 500 ms clears the composer, and the row says `esc again to clear`, because nothing else in this phase discards a draft without a key that says so; at an empty idle composer Escape arms nothing (`app_input_runtime.zig:3210-3213`). A prompt submitted while a turn is running is upstream's steer, delivered into that turn at upstream's boundaries (`worker_runtime.zig:1080-1104`, `orchestrator.zig:157-261`): over a tool-free answer at once -- the answer cut where it had got to and kept as the assistant's message -- while a tool runs after its results, and with the model's answer after it, the turn going on; the model is told each in upstream's `<user_steering>` wrapper and a later turn replays the words as typed. It is the band's `┋ <prompt>` row in the dim grey until the turn takes it and its card is written then; Up at an empty composer takes the newest back while it still waits for a tool (`popQueuedSteerForEdit`); a stop drops steers with the queue, and one the turn never took runs as the next prompt. A request the turn retries (a status the server says is transient, a connection that never opened) replaces the activity row with upstream's recovery label, `⚠ Provider unavailable · HTTP 500 · server_error: upstream exploded · retrying request in 1s`, counting down to the next attempt and kept through a retried answer arriving, then `✓ recovered · attempt N` for 1.5 s or until the turn ends (`worker_status.zig:5`, `:221-235`), a label too wide for the screen wrapping onto up to three rows under its mark (`render_input.zig:406`, `:689-697`); the retry budget is xfx's own three attempts, not upstream's (re-verified against fx v0.0.16, `481104fd`, which changes nothing a retry shows: upstream's label, `recoveryLabel`, `src/core/shared/types.zig:539-606@481104fd`, differs from 0.0.13's only by an action the retry path never takes, and its count still rounds up, `format_retry_label`, `src/core/output/worker_status.zig:201-219`, `:212@481104fd` -- xfx's `src/tui/retry.rs:369-381`, `:384-391`, with its three causes at `:252-261`; the statuses retried are still 429, 500, 502, 503 and 504, `fromHttpStatus`, `src/core/agent/stream_provider.zig:480-492`, and `isRetryableModelFailure`, `src/core/agent/runtime/orchestrator.zig:4496-4501@481104fd`, as xfx's `src/gateway/mod.rs:994-995`; 0.0.16's one change on that path, `fe7925b2`, deletes a stall stop a status failure could never reach; and upstream's turn still never ends on a transient failure, backing off 250 ms, 1 s, 2 s and on to 30 s and slowing to once a minute after fifteen minutes, `src/core/agent/runtime/model_response_recovery.zig:5-9`, `:270-282@481104fd`, where xfx's three attempts wait 250 and then 500 ms, `src/gateway/mod.rs:59`, `src/agent/machine.rs:78-85`, `:199-207`), and Escape over a retry arms the interrupt because upstream pauses only its unbudgeted wait for connectivity, which xfx does not have. That decoder is the session's own: bytes in, keystrokes out, one machine fed in arrival order (the keystrokes the launch probe read with the cursor report included), with a lone `ESC` resolved as the Escape key only after 50 ms of quiet, an unfinished `CSI` waited for however long the terminal takes, an unknown sequence bounded at 32 bytes and reported as a keystroke with no binding rather than as a phantom Escape, `ESC` followed by a control byte delivered as the Escape key *and* that byte -- except `ESC DEL`, which is Alt-Backspace and deletes back over one word of word characters, as upstream's does (`composer_deletion.zig:103-136`) where `C-w` deletes back to whitespace, and `ESC CR`/`ESC LF`, which is Alt-Enter and inserts a newline (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :515-519), because replaying either would make two of them two Escapes inside the double-Esc window and throw the whole draft away, and an Alt-Enter a submit besides -- and **no** C0 byte or control scalar ever delivered as text -- a tab included, because a control that reached the composer would reach the terminal again on submit; a tab is a *named keystroke* instead, which the approval panel walks its choices with and the composer has no binding for. **Key reports are decoded in both of their spellings** -- kitty's `CSI <key> ; <mods> u` and `modifyOtherKeys`' `CSI 27 ; <mods> ; <key> ~` -- through one port of upstream's `kittyUnicodeKeyAction` (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :185-266), in its branch order: a Ctrl letter is that letter's control byte and therefore its binding, Escape, Enter with a modifier, Alt-Backspace, Alt-b/f, Super+Z and the keypad are the keys they already are here, Caps Lock and Num Lock are not modifiers, and a key **release** is never a keystroke; Super+A, Super+C and Super+X are the composer's selection (:223-231) -- select the whole draft, copy it, cut it, through the clipboard `/copy` uses, the copy silent and the cut taking the text out only once the clipboard has it (`cutSelection`, `input_selection_runtime.zig:32-42`), the selected run painted in reverse video (`input_presentation.zig:995-1021`); Alt-d deletes the word to the right; Ctrl+O opens the full transcript; and a page key, bare or held with a modifier (`pageKeyAction`, :169-173), pages whichever surface has the keys -- the full transcript while it is open, the list of an open menu or picker by the composer's row cap (`routeComposerVertical`'s `picker_delta`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:222-233@481104fd`; `routeHelpMenuMove`, `:465-483`), and otherwise the composer's caret by the same number of rows (`fx-p5-input-row-limit-41x120-at-pgdn`, `-help-pgdn`, `-slash-pgdn`, `-grown-pgup`). Three of fx's lists that page so take no page key here yet: `/settings`' menu (`routeSettingsMenuMove`, `:431-463@481104fd`); the `/model ` column with its effort and speed stages (`navigateModelPicker`, `:1072-1096@481104fd`); and `/mcp `'s argument completions, which fx counts as a slash list (`slashCompletionCount` over the registry's `arguments`, `command_specs.zig:578-585`, `:876-889@481104fd`; those are `/mcp`'s verbs and servers, `main.zig:441-445`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:459-468@481104fd`) and so pages by `picker_delta` (`input_completion_runtime.zig:255-260@481104fd`). xfx binds no page key on any of them: `SettingsAction::of` names none (`src/tui/settings.rs:539-551`), nor does the column's key set (`offer_to_column`'s `bound`, `src/tui/shell.rs`), nor `offer_to_mcp_args`. Owner: PAGE-KEYS-SETTINGS-COLUMN (the dispatcher's queue). `/mcp`'s menu takes a page key as one step, as fx's does (`routeMcpHostMenuMove`, `:424-429@481104fd`). What upstream resolves to a key xfx still does not have -- a selection-extending move, spelled on a letter (Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F, Alt+Shift+B/F) or with Shift on a page key, and the all-sessions toggle (`open_all_sessions`, :253) -- binds nothing beyond the plain move: Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F bind nothing, and Alt+Shift+B/F are the word moves with the Shift dropped, as the arrows' is (`escape_parser.zig:244`, `:248@481104fd`; a question's draft drops it in fx too, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:155-197@481104fd`), until the composer has a selection to extend (owner unit **COMPOSER-SELECTION-EXTEND**, queued); Shift-Tab, upstream's `toggle_permission_mode` (:254), is the permission-mode cycle (the `/permissions` row). A selection-extending **arrow** is the exception and is not a dead key: kitty's Up and Down (`57352`/`57353`) and the keypad's arrows, Home and End resolve through xfx's own cursor table, which drops Shift together with the selection it would have extended, so with Shift held -- alone or beside Ctrl or Alt -- the key makes the plain move it makes here unshifted (a word move for Left and Right under Ctrl or Alt) where upstream would extend a selection; the legacy `CSI 1 ; <mod> A` spelling takes the same table. **Super with an arrow is a one-character or one-row step here, where upstream jumps**: Super-Left and -Right to the line's start and end, Super-Up and -Down to the draft's (`modifiedArrowAction`, `escape_parser.zig:74-82@481104fd`; an approval's amendment the same, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:82-83`); xfx's cursor table reads Super on Left and Right as the step (the decoder's `ComposerLeft`/`ComposerRight`, which only an approval's choice tells apart) and on Up and Down as the arrow (the decoder's `DraftStart`/`DraftEnd`, which only an approval tells apart: its amendment takes them to the draft's edges, as upstream's does). Owner unit: **COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS** (queued). Every key herdr 0.9.3 (libghostty-vt, kitty flag 1) was captured sending in that form decodes to its binding, and its `u`-form Ctrl-U and Ctrl-D are driven end to end on a real pty (`.prd/tui-phase3/receipts/2026-10-06-herdr-csi-u-keys.md`). The grammar is stricter than upstream's -- one canonical decimal spelling per field, no extra field -- and not looser anywhere. Bracketed paste is framed: `?2004h` is enabled, the bytes between `ESC[200~` and `ESC[201~` are content rather than keys -- so a newline inside a paste does not submit it, a `0x03` does not cancel a turn, and an escape sequence is not obeyed -- and anything over 1000 codepoints is shown in the composer as `[Pasted text #N, M lines]` (`1 line` for one) and expanded back into the prompt when it is sent -- the prompt's card, the turn's estimate and a steer's card are that expanded text, every line on rows of its own, as upstream's card is (`input_submit_runtime.zig:705-706`, `user_message_card.zig:348-383`, `orchestrator.zig:5482-5485`) -- expanded to the text the frame made of it, which is the pasted bytes filtered, decoded as UTF-8 with anything that is not UTF-8 replaced, and with CR and CRLF line breaks normalized to LF, rather than to the bytes the terminal sent. **A prompt's card is shown in the frame that clears the composer**, as fx v0.0.16's 70611a91 shows one (`submit` queues the card in the `route_bytes` that takes the draft, `src/tui/shell.rs` `send`/`echo`): whole, when it fits between the document's last row and fx's fixed content bottom, `rows - 4` (`footer_rows`, `src/main.zig:173@481104fd`; `p5-transcript-submit` wire, the first frame after Enter); otherwise only its newest rows -- as many as that room holds, at least one, and no more than the frame's band leaves under the row above the card and the blank row over the activity row -- at the band's top, and the card itself, whole, in the next frame, once that one has landed (`buildPendingCardProjection`, `PendingCardPaintContext`, `transcriptAreaBeforePendingTail`, `noteCommittedFrame`: `src/core/app/app_render_runtime.zig:168-277`, `:2230-2238`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:289-307@481104fd`; `Shell::preview_room`, `preview_card`, `preview_landed`). A three-line prompt on a full 40-row screen previews its last line, five lines their last three, a 60-line pasted block its last 32 rows under the welcome (`p5-transcript-submit-multi`, `p5-submit-preview-*` wires; `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/submit-card-preview/`). The preview is the band's rows, not the document's, so none of it reaches scrollback, and every screen after it is the one the card was written onto before. The card's frame follows the preview's in the same tick, with nothing read and no resize taken between them, as fx writes the card in its next frame once its preview's has committed (`noteCommittedFrame`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:289-307@481104fd`; `Shell::take_preview_shown`). What stays open is a write that fails, as it does between fx's two writes: the card's frame refused or torn before its rows, or the preview's frame torn, and then a screen that shrinks before the retry -- the preview's rows the terminal moves into its history, or keeps above the band where they are the document's to xfx after a resize, stay there, and the card is still written once, whole and in order (`a_failed_write_and_a_shrink_before_the_retry_leave_only_what_the_shrink_took`). The room is measured against the last landed frame, as fx measures from its last committed one (`src/ui/transcript/runtime.zig:9929-9932@481104fd`; five lines and their Enter in one read preview what the one-row composer's frame left, `p5-submit-preview-oneread`). Checked against fx at 40x120, 30x100 and 24x80. Written whole at once, where fx would still preview (its pending path is shut only by an open stream, paced output, Ctrl-O or a turn start its worker will not hold, `installPendingSubmission`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:1123-1150@481104fd`), so xfx previews less than fx there, with no capture of fx's frame for them: a card under a line that does not end on a blank row (fx's mid-line endpoint, `pendingCardLeadingAdvanceRows`, `src/core/app/app_render_runtime.zig:209-218`); under a fork's `original session` line (`raw_gap`); under a tool group still open; one submitted while the document still owes rows, a reset's welcome among them; on a screen of 8 rows or fewer, whose running band leaves the preview no row (`room` 0, where fx may still paint one row at its band's bottom, `PendingCardPaintContext.init`, `:185-187`); a card whose preview the band loses the room for before the preview's frame (the slash menu opened by a key read with the Enter, a steer's banner): written whole that frame, the preview gone for good; a resize before the preview's frame (the preview is dropped); a card sent after a resize and before a band frame of the new size has landed, which leaves nothing to measure from (fx measures from its last committed frame, `runtime.zig:9929-9932`, then one of the old size); a question or Ctrl-O taking the other plane before the preview's frame: the card is paid whole before `1049h`, as the barrier pays every row the primary plane owes (fx paints no pending card while the alternate screen owns rendering, `:1264-1268`); and the exit. The activity row under it is xfx's own, as under every card (T7). A pasted prompt taller than the screen reaches scrollback whole, every line once and in order, as the session's first prompt too (433464dd, `p5-transcript-tallfirst`). A collapsed block is an **atomic entity**: it is a *span* of the draft rather than a name to be searched for, so exactly one run of the draft is the block and a second copy of its summary -- typed, or pasted back off the screen, before it or after it -- is only ever the words it looks like. Every motion steps over it whole (left, right, both word moves, and the two vertical moves, which land in front of it rather than inside it, as upstream's land on a unit's start), so the caret is never inside a summary; every delete that overlaps it at all takes the whole of it, so one backspace at its right edge, one forward delete at its left edge, a word delete or either kill removes the block rather than damaging its name. The span moves with the text around it as that text is edited. A number a block was given is **never** given again in one session -- a removed block's number is spent -- and a line recalled from history brings its blocks back under **fresh** numbers, allocated from the same checked counter a paste spends, which is what keeps two live blocks from answering to one name; at the end of that counter a paste is refused visibly and a recall hands the line back as words and says so, rather than wrapping or reusing a number. Removing a block gives its bytes back to the budget for good: what was pasted stops being sent, and typing the same words in again is typing rather than repairing. **A paste ends with its delivery, as fx v0.0.16's does.** A delivery is one continuously readable run of input: every read until a readiness check finds nothing more -- one read or many, since a read takes at most 128 bytes -- and across the 32-read cap of one turn, which carries it into the next turn unsettled (`event_loop.zig:94-119@481104fd`). Within one delivery, input that arrives behind the end marker is taken for the clipboard's rather than the user's, and it is **never read as keys**: it is counted and dropped, a second end marker or a new paste's start included. The paste it followed is **kept**, and upstream's `! input: Input that arrived right after the paste was ignored.` is said once it has been (`paste_framing.zig:58-68,102-107,159-165`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:168-198@481104fd`, 9e0eab41; c1db919 refused the whole paste instead). So clipboard text carrying an `ESC[201~` and a newline, delivered in one go, cannot submit the composer, answer an approval or take a question's choice. Every byte there is the clipboard's whatever its origin: a readiness check cannot tell a key a person pressed from a byte the terminal is still writing. **Past that, xfx diverges from fx, for security: the input stays quarantined until it has been quiet for 50 ms.** fx's delivery is the terminal's, not the clipboard's: a paste longer than the pty's input queue (1022 bytes on macOS) arrives as the terminal refills it, a check can find the queue empty in between, and fx settles the paste there and reads the rest of the clipboard as keys (`event_loop.zig:94-119@481104fd`). Measured against a real xfx at fx's boundary (the PASTE-GAP receipt, `measure.txt`): on a plain pty the paste's own newline submitted it in 2 of 3 runs at 64 KiB and at 1 MiB, and through tmux on a terminal that does not strip the marker in every run from 4 KiB up -- tmux ends a paste at its first embedded `ESC[201~` and forwards the rest as keys (`tty-keys.c:213-214,641-646@7f2a35ad`). And no terminal layer can be relied on to strip it: kitty, Alacritty, VTE, foot and Windows Terminal do; WezTerm strips once, so a nested marker survives; xterm, iTerm2 and tmux's `paste-buffer -S` let the user turn the filter off; Terminal.app cannot be checked (`terminals.md` there). So the paste is still settled where fx settles it -- the same `PasteEnd`, at the same check, so every capture is fx's -- and after it every byte is counted and dropped like the bytes behind the marker, until two checks 50 ms apart find nothing with nothing read between them: a fact about the queue, not the clock, so a loop that stalls between two reads of a paste still reads it as one. The warning is said at the first check after anything was dropped, and **while dropped input keeps arriving it is said again once a second**, in xfx's own words saying what holds the quarantine then (`Decoder::PASTE_REPEAT`, `Hold`): `! input: Keys ignored until input stops.` for the quiet, `! input: Paste still open; keys are ignored.` while a paste begun behind the end marker is open, and `! input: Key sequence open; keys are ignored.` while a marker is read past its first byte -- the two framing sentences name the cause and no release point, so they stay true however many keys continue the sequence and whatever ends the open paste; each of the three wraps into two rows of at most 18 columns, and fx's own warning into three, so each is whole on the review plane's three status rows down to the narrowest screen a session has, 20 columns, and a screen too short for all of its rows gives each of these four up whole rather than cut, because a cut of them says something else ("Input that arrived right after the", "Keys ignored until") -- every other status on the plane is given up from its last row, as before, so a refusal keeps the first clause that says which refusal it is for as long as there is a row for it; on that plane the sentence is the status row, and at the end of every turn's input -- after the burst, however it ended, and before the frame -- it is taken down once it no longer names what holds the quarantine: the hold has changed, the quarantine has ended, or a paste has replaced it with a new one in which nothing has been said; nothing new is said in its place, because the notices stay rate-limited and the plane's line is a view of now -- fx's own warning about input after a paste, and any other status, stay; and once a repeat has named a hold, a change in what holds the quarantine is said even with nothing dropped, so `ESC [` alone after the quiet's sentence is said in its own words -- at a check that finds nothing and at a turn's 32-read cap alike, so a stream that never pauses is said too (`Decoder::still`); on the plane the user can see, as the first warning is. fx has no such line because it has no quarantine.
