Enter sends **only** the draft belonging to the answer it takes and discards the other; Escape and Ctrl-C are the fail-safe exits and send none at all, which is the one place they differ from `3`. It reaches the model as a user message **after** every tool result of its step, and it is recorded as a `tool_feedback` frame keyed by call id -- not as a `user_message`, which would open a turn in the middle of a tool batch. Opening, closing or growing a draft revokes the readiness receipt and the next disclosed frame re-earns it; the draft is paid for out of what is left after the target, the controls and the scope, so it can shorten only itself. **The sentence is read back in the transcript** as upstream reads it back, the user's own prompt card -- `┃ ` and the sentence in bold, a row per line of it, and a blank row under it (`write_user_prompt`, `app_worker_runtime.zig:1020-1026`) -- under the tool group's rows, live and on a resumed session alike, and a prompt in the full transcript (C-o). It is written when the runtime reports the delivery rather than when the key was pressed, so the document can never claim something was said that was not: an interrupted draft is on the screen while it is being typed and reaches neither the transcript, the journal nor the wire. That is the one place it can differ from upstream's: upstream writes the card as soon as that call's own result is in (`appendPermissionFeedbackAfterToolResult`, `orchestrator.zig:5360-5380`, from `:11379-11387`), before the step's later calls have run, and xfx delivers every sentence of a step after its last result, so in a step of several calls the card comes after the step's last result rather than its own call's; in a step of one call the two are the same place. A `write_file` carries the same bounded before/after an edit does, built in the executor from the preimage it has already read a complete-read proof for and the exact text that would replace it, so the largest change this product makes is reviewed as a change rather than as a digest; a target with nothing at it is reviewed as all additions, `-0` in the header, and one holding bytes that are not UTF-8 says what they are and how many rather than being rendered lossily as replacement characters. `create_folder` changes no content and still has no pair to show. Off this surface nothing changes: a run with no terminal on both ends still has no approval channel and still denies every `ask`-mode mutation with the refusal a pipe gets. A panic inside a turn never touches the terminal: the runtime thread neither restores it nor reports into it -- the panic arrives as data, and the UI restores, prints it **once**, and exits nonzero. **The TUI answers the same slash commands the line-oriented shell does, and no others**, from that shell's own `classify` and its `SLASH_REGISTRY`, so a leading `/` cannot mean two different things depending on which front end it was typed into: `/help`, `/version`, `/quit` and `/stats` are answered by the UI thread, and `/status` and `/copy` from what the runtime publishes; `/model` and `/setup` are handed to the runtime thread as work, because the model, the conversation and the provider they change live there -- and each of them has to change *between* turns rather than under one; `/clear`, `/new` and `/reset` are one reset, sent as a control message that cancels the running turn and drops the conversation once it has unwound, so a reset never waits behind a turn or takes a queue place from the prompt typed after it; `/permissions` and Shift-Tab are answered by the UI thread and hand their change to the runtime on a channel of their own, which it applies between pieces of work. A name that is not one of the seven is refused with the same sentence that shell refuses it with and is **not** sent to the model, so a mistyped command does not quietly become a prompt. A reset's screen half is upstream's scrollback handoff (`commitVisibleTranscriptBeforeFreshSession`): the band's rows are erased, the document's rows still on the screen are scrolled off the top into the terminal's own scrollback -- `CUP` to the bottom row and one linefeed per row, the same instrument every append uses, and not the blank row the document ends on, which upstream does not carry either -- and the welcome line is written on row 1 of the blank screen with the band floated under it; a question that owns the other buffer defers it until the plane is given back. Answer rows that had already landed when a running turn was reset go to scrollback with the rest of the document, where upstream, which holds an answer in its live region until it is committed, carries only the prompt card. A draft that is one word beginning with `/` also opens an **inline completion menu** in the same rows of the band a question would take -- never both, because a question closes a menu before it appears. The menu is a view of the composer rather than a second focus: the caret stays in the text being typed, only five keys are taken out of the stream (Up, Down, Tab, Enter, Esc; and `C-k` and `C-j` as Up and Down, except under the full transcript, which holds over the menu and where they are the composer's -- `routePickerControlByte`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1093-1116@481104fd`; `p5-ctrlo-slash`, CTRLO-OTHER-PICKERS), and Enter runs the line rather than taking the marked row, so no command costs two Returns. Matches are ranked name-prefix, then alias-prefix, then substring, ties broken by the order `/help` lists them, so the rows are a pure function of what was typed; Tab completes the marked command, with a trailing space for the one that takes an argument; Esc closes the menu without arming the composer's own double-Escape clear, and the closing survives further letters of the same word. The skills the query finds follow its commands, and a slash word that ends a longer draft has the rest of a command's name painted after the caret instead, for Right or Tab to take (the `/skills` row). Upstream's fuzzy matching, its categories and its argument completion are absent. The word has to start the draft: fx opens the menu for a draft whose first character past any leading spaces, tabs or line breaks is `/` (`inlinePickerTriggerKind`, `src/core/input/picker_state.zig:293-294@481104fd`; `slashCompletionPrefix`, `command_specs.zig:568-576`), where xfx's `Trigger::of` asks for the `/` as the first character (`src/tui/picker.rs:165-167`), so `  /he` or a slash word after a blank line opens fx's menu and not xfx's. Uncaptured; owner SLASH-LEADING-WS (found by PAGE-KEYS-SETTINGS-COLUMN's review, not fixed there). **Every line the composer submits is remembered, and walking back through them never costs the line being typed.** A hundred entries, newest first, with adjacent repeats folded into one and commands kept beside prompts -- a command is a line the user typed, and running one again is the commonest reason to reach for a recall; a blank line is the one submission that is not recorded, because there is nothing to come back to. Up and Down are the composer's own movement first, take the caret to the draft's start or end at its first or last visual row, and become the walk only from there (`input_completion_runtime.zig:265-284`), so a multi-row draft is still navigable by arrow, and `C-n` steps forward from wherever the caret is (upstream's `history_next`, `shortcuts.zig:17`). `C-p` is not the backward walk: it is upstream's model catalog (`open_model_catalog`, `escape_parser.zig:271`), the menu `/model` opens, with the draft lent out of the composer -- through the effort column a choice in it goes on into -- and given back as it was, text, blocks and caret, when the flow ends: a model applied, Escape, a second `C-p`, `C-c` or `C-d` (`app_input_runtime.zig:2356-2404`, `:1666-1676`, `:2775-2779`); over the `/help` catalog or the `/statusline` picker it does nothing (`:2406-2418`). A recalled line that begins with `/` opens no completion menu until it is edited, so a further Up keeps walking (`picker_state.zig:451-466`). Entering the walk **captures the half-typed line**, and stepping forward past the newest entry hands it back, so a recall is not a gesture that destroys what was being written. Neither end wraps: past the oldest line is nothing, and the near end is the captured draft. An edit ends the walk and the edited text is what the next one comes back to; a caret move does not, because reading a recalled prompt before stepping further back is exactly what an arrow key is for there. Nothing is written to disk and nothing survives the process: a session recall rather than a history file, which is the boundary the `prompt history` row records. An entry carries the collapsed pastes its summaries named, so a recalled line comes back as the **blocks** rather than as words -- under fresh paste numbers, which is upstream's renumbering and is what keeps the recalled line and the draft it replaced from both answering to `#1`. The draft a walk stands aside carries its blocks the same way, so the half-typed line the walk comes back to is the whole of it. This phase adds no command, no tool and no canonical slash name -- it adds one alias, `/exit`, on the `/quit` row. **One thing is named here because it is absent from every stable release.** A stateless per-vector output check -- every vector the TUI is about to write decoded into a model of the terminal and compared, **before** `write_all` rather than after it, against an intent its emitter declares separately from the bytes: the whole model as a value (plane, cursor visibility, the shown title and the title stack, each mode as a tri-state that distinguishes *nothing has said* from a guessed default, whether scrollback was erased, the queries asked, the saved cursor), the cells against a grid built from the emitter's own inputs with colour compared through a parser of its own on each side, and a document append replayed in emission order so a row that scrolls off the top is compared **before** the scroll evicts it -- is **absent from stable releases**; preview-channel builds run it unconditionally, with no opt-in flag (`.prd/03-tui-port.md` item 21). **The transport those vectors go out on is absent from stable releases in the same way**: preview-channel builds write every byte of a session through one unbuffered counted sink -- no buffer and no flush, so there is no vector a session believes it wrote that is still held in one -- which adds up what the kernel accepted and tells a refusal and a write that took *nothing* (the screen is as it was; the vector is still owed, and the half-second budget above is what ends a run of them) apart from a write the screen took **part** of (it is not, so the session ends on it at once, on a terminal the exit still puts back, rather than offering the same vector again and writing that prefix twice). That is **containment**: an accepted count is a kernel receipt rather than a terminal's acknowledgement, and nothing here claims a parser recovered. Partial-write recovery covers the two repaints whose whole intended content is in hand -- the primary band's frame, and a repaint of the alternate plane a question already owns -- each with one fixed cleanup and one whole rebuild of the same repaint in the same call, never a replay of its bytes, and no approval readiness until a later write the session verifies on its own; every other `Partial` -- a plane transition, a document append or carry, a reset's scrollback handoff, the theme query, the retint -- is contained and fatal -- the session ends and leaves its give-up diagnostic -- and its bytes are never replayed (the table under this one gives each path and its reason), though a `Rejected`/`ZeroProgress` write -- an append or a refused handoff alike -- is retained and reoffered under the existing budget rather than dropped. A released binary has none of it (`.prd/03-tui-port.md` item 21; [`.prd/06-qa-harness.md`](../.prd/06-qa-harness.md) rows 3c, 3d and 3e drive the containment and both recoveries on a real pseudoterminal with the fault build). Without it -- and for any other value of it, for every subcommand, and whenever either end is not a terminal -- xfx is the line-oriented shell of the `interactive` row and changes no terminal state. **Widths a terminal disputes, in an answer and in the composer.** The painter counts a scalar a terminal was measured drawing wider than the width table counted it, where fx counts it wider too, as fx counts it (3,838 of them, `src/tui/wrap.rs`'s `FX_COUNTED`; the same cells as fx's in `p5-ctrlo-wider-inline`). Regional indicators are counted as fx counts them, which is not as the terminals draw them: a pair that is no flag four cells (drawn two), a lone one two (one on tmux), and a run a grapheme cluster at a time where fx pairs it along the line (Z U S six, fx four). A document row ends early and loses nothing; where the grid rewrites only the cells that changed, a cell the count put elsewhere can stay on the screen (`ab` of an erased `🇦🇦abcd` in the composer, fx's and xfx's alike) -- the ledger's cause `ri-run-pairing`, RI-COUNT's. The 43 fx and xfx count alike and a terminal draws wider (`src/tui/drawn_wider.rs`) are **named in an answer's rows** -- prose, a code panel and its label, a table's cells, operational text; a base with a skin-tone modifier is named past the base and its modifier stays (`\u{261d}🏻`, whole on both terminals); display only, the journal and `/copy`'s reply keep the model's bytes -- where fx writes them as themselves and its rows lose glyphs on tmux 3.7b and libghostty-vt 1.3.2 (`answer-drawn-wider-named`, unsafe-to-copy; `p5-ctrlo-wider-inline-fam`, `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/ctrlo-wider/`). **The draft is written as fx writes it** and loses glyphs as fx's does (`y a` of `fam ☝ ⸺ 🖐 x` `U+E0002` `y a` `U+FE0F` `b` on tmux, four `U+261D` in front costing its tail): the user's own text, a decision the ledger's cause `composer-drawn-wider` records, owned by COMPOSER-WIDER (display-only naming with a caret map; a file name an `@` completion puts in the draft is the route a file's text could take, PLAUSIBLE). The card of a sent prompt is the user's text too, written as it stands as a document row, where every glyph stays (fx's card loses `y a` to the anchor after `a` `U+FE0F`: `inline-band-row-anchor`). |

-=======
-| full-screen TUI | ui | partial | Opt-in and narrower than upstream's. `XFX_TUI=1` on a bare `xfx` takes the terminal into raw mode on its **normal** buffer: the band, the transcript and the composer live there for the life of the session and never move, and mouse reporting is never enabled, so the terminal's own scrollback and wheel keep working. The alternate screen is an owned excursion and nothing else -- `\x1b[?1049h` is written only to review a change too big for the band to show, for as long as that question is unanswered (below), and for the full transcript `C-o` opens, for as long as it is open (its own row below); a session that does neither, however long, writes it never. Under tmux the kitty keyboard push is omitted, because sending it there breaks key input. Raw mode is an obligation rather than a feature: the entry bits (`ECHO`/`ICANON`/`IEXTEN`/`ISIG` clear, `CS8`, `VMIN=1`, `VTIME=0`) are asserted positively while the child runs, and the `termios` captured before entry is put back on exit -- onto the descriptor it was captured from, and whether or not the screen could still be written. The band is a divider, the composer, and a hint row at the bottom of the screen -- with one more row above the divider while a turn is running, saying what it is doing, and a block of rows between the two while that turn is waiting to be told whether it may change something; every frame is one write wrapped in synchronized output (`?2026h`/`l`) -- a frame that also grows the document carries those rows inside the same update, ahead of the band, as upstream's frame carries its movement bytes (`composeWireFrame`, `src/ui/render_engine/terminal_diff.zig:604-645` at `c1db919`), so a terminal that honours the mode never presents a new row with the band still where the scroll left it -- and what that write carries is the **difference** between what the terminal is holding and what it should be holding: the band keeps a shadow grid of grapheme cells, wide-cell continuations and attribute state, builds the grid the frame would leave, and emits a `CUP`, replacement text and an `EL` for the rows that changed and for nothing else -- so a keystroke costs the cell it changed and an animation phase costs the digit it turned over. **A frame whose cells, window title and caret are all unchanged writes zero bytes**, which is what keeps an idle session silent between the loop's 8 ms ticks. The shadow is advanced only by a write that succeeded, so a refused frame is owed again rather than believed; a Ctrl-L and the resume after a `SIGTSTP` mark the screen externally damaged, and the frame after either opens with the Phase-1 erase -- the rows a shrinking band gave back, then `CUP` to the band's top row and `ED` -- because a forgotten shadow is *unknown* rather than blank, and a diff that treated it as blank would rewrite the band's own columns and leave whatever the shell put beside them on every band row. The erase starts at the band's top row and never above it, so the terminal's own document survives a resume. A resume also re-arms the window title: the restore that hands the terminal back pops the title stack, so the title the session set is gone, and the band owes a fresh `OSC 2` -- one push per time the terminal is taken and one pop per time it is given back, so a stop/resume cycle leaves the stack balanced. The optimization is judged by the screen it leaves rather than by the bytes it saves: the `fault-injection` build keeps the Phase-1 whole-band painter behind a compile-time seam (`Fault::FullPaintReference`, absent from every released binary), and `scripts/smoke-tui.sh`'s scenario 13 drives identical facts through both painters on a real pseudoterminal and requires the final cells, attributes and caret to be equal. **The terminal's window title is set** -- `OSC 2 ; xfx · <model>`, with every control stripped out of the model label so a configured name cannot close the sequence and have the rest of itself executed -- and it is borrowed rather than taken: the mode set pushes the terminal's own title onto its stack (`CSI 22 ; 2 t`) and every restore path, normal and abnormal, pops it back (`CSI 23 ; 2 t`). A title is written as its own `OSC 2` right ahead of the frame's synchronized update and never inside it, which is where upstream's lands: upstream writes a title the moment it changes, as a write of its own between frames (`syncTerminalTitleWith`, `src/core/app/app_session_runtime.zig:3608-3629`; `setTerminalTitleLabel`, `src/ui/render.zig:723-733` at `c1db919`), so its bytes reach the terminal after one frame's `?2026l` and before the next one's `?2026h`; the output check refuses an update led by anything else, or by a title other than the one the band owes. The line-oriented shell sets no title and pushes nothing. **What has already reached the terminal's own scrollback is never rewritten**: the diff writes only at or below the band's top row, plus the rows a shrinking band gave back, and a document append still scrolls the screen with real linefeeds -- committed rows stay the terminal's. A terminal smaller than 6 rows or 20 columns is refused with a reason rather than painted over. **What those rows say**: the divider spans the screen, the composer shows what has been typed into it behind its prompt marker, and the hint row says what a turn would be run with -- in upstream's order, joined by upstream's separator: a call to action when the configured provider has no usable credential (a Gateway with no bearer token or a llmux with no URL -- both refuse every turn, and the row says so before a prompt is spent on one), the permission mode, and a compact model label that drops the provider prefix and the `claude-` family prefix. A refusal takes the left of it whole when one has just happened, and `esc again to clear` sits flush with the last column while the double-Esc gesture is armed, the row's colours closed before its padding as upstream's are (`input_presentation.zig:551-567`). On a screen too narrow for all of it the segments are **dropped whole, from the right**, rather than clipped mid-number: the permission mode gives way in front of the model rather than the model behind it, and the right-hand warning is reserved before the left is measured, because it is about a key that is already armed. The meter upstream ends that row with is here too and is filled from a completed turn's own `input_tokens` over the context window the provider's catalog publishes, shown only when the session has both numbers and dropped whole rather than defaulted when it has one; what is still absent is the shortcut legend. **While a turn is in flight there is one row above the divider**, and only then: it says `• Thinking` with how long the turn has been running, or names the tool that is running instead of saying `Thinking` at all -- a turn that has gone quiet because a tool is taking a minute otherwise looks exactly like a turn that has gone quiet. The marker blinks every 500 ms, counted in the renderer's own 50 ms animation phases rather than on a second clock beside it, and the row asks for a frame when its text changes rather than on every phase, so a band whose row says the same thing is not repainted twenty times a second and an idle band asks for nothing at all. The row is the **band's** while it exists: it takes its row from the bottom of the document exactly as a composer that grew by one does, and the divider, the composer and the caret do not move when a turn starts. It is **exactly the turn's lifetime**: the runtime says when a turn begins and says when it ends, in order on the one channel that carries everything else it says, and the band draws the row between the two. Told rather than counted, and that is correctness rather than taste -- a prompt is accepted the moment it is typed and may then wait behind another turn for a minute (the hint row is where waiting is said, and a waiting prompt gets no row), and the place a finished turn holds is given back *after* its conclusion is sent, so a band counting places would announce a turn that had not started and start a clock for one that no longer exists. **The clock is one turn's**: a prompt queued behind a running one does not restart it, and when that turn ends the row begins again from zero for whatever turn the runtime starts next rather than carrying the finished one's elapsed time forward. A `/model` travels on the same channel but is not a turn, and no row appears for one. Two things named rather than implied: the elapsed clock **stops while xfx is waiting for a decision**, because that interval measures the person rather than the model -- the approval panel below is what pends one, and the row itself is off the band while a question is up, as upstream's is (`render_input.zig:705`), and comes back at the elapsed time it stopped at; and the token count upstream shows beside the elapsed time is **absent**, because the streamed event set carries no usage -- the row says nothing about tokens rather than reporting a zero nobody measured. What is *above* the divider is the turn, described below. The band's size is asked of **standard output**, because that is the screen it is drawn on; raw mode is entered on standard input, because that is where input arrives; and a redirected session whose two ends are different terminals gets no cursor report at all, so it starts at row 1 and pushes nothing. A screen that refuses every frame for half a second of wall-clock time ends the session with the first of those errors, on a terminal the exit still puts back, rather than being retried invisibly forever; the budget is measured on a clock rather than in repaints, because the 8 ms tick is the longest a turn waits and not the shortest. The exit clears from the band's top row downward (`app_lifecycle.zig:578-593`), and only when a band was really painted: a session that left before its first frame -- a Ctrl-D that arrived with the cursor report -- moves no cursor and erases nothing. At launch it asks the terminal where the cursor is (CSI `6n`, 100 ms deadline, row 1 if the terminal does not answer; the row under it when the cursor is past the first column) and pushes the shell output above that row into scrollback -- or, with `/settings`' `Startup scrollback` off, starts on that row under it and pushes only what leaves fewer than eleven document rows (`app_lifecycle.zig:944-988`) -- moving the cursor to the bottom row first, because a linefeed scrolls a terminal only from the bottom margin and from anywhere else merely walks the cursor down -- so the band never paints over what was already on screen; a keystroke typed while the query is in flight is deferred, not swallowed. Restoration is proven one case per exit path, each on a real pty: the normal exit, `SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP`/an external `SIGINT` (the child dies *by the signal*, which is what proves the handler reset the disposition and re-raised rather than fabricating a status) -- **except a `SIGINT` the process was started ignoring** (`nohup … &`), which stays ignored, so a `kill -INT` changes nothing and every command the session runs inherits the ignore, as upstream's TUI catches no `SIGINT` at all (`installAbnormalExitHandlers` owns `SIGTERM` and `SIGHUP` only, `app_lifecycle.zig:82-104` at `c1db9191`) -- and `SIGTSTP`/`SIGCONT` including a **second** `SIGTSTP` after a resume, which is what proves the stop handler was reinstalled. Every one of those is an ordinary transition rather than a race, because the owned signals are blocked across the whole of it -- from before the `tcsetattr` that takes the terminal until after the last handler is installed -- so a supervisor that signals xfx during startup finds a handler rather than a default disposition and a raw terminal. The signals named here are the ones a *supervisor or an operator* sends: with `ISIG` cleared a **typed** Ctrl-C or Ctrl-Z generates no signal at all, and arrives as a byte for the input decoder. Ctrl-C is a byte, not a signal, and it means what upstream's semantic Ctrl-C means (`app_input_runtime.zig:1675-1712` at `c1db919`): over a running turn with a draft in the composer it takes the draft and nothing else; otherwise every press is a press of one three-second exit window -- the first arms it, says `press ctrl+c again to exit` on the hint row for as long as it lasts (the `/help` catalog's row included), and stops the running turn or throws the idle draft away; a second inside it leaves with **130**, disjoint from the external `SIGINT` above, which still kills the process by the signal unless the launcher ignored it; any other keystroke closes the window early. A stopped turn leaves upstream's line, `▪ Cancelled · What can xfx do differently?`, at the keystroke: the answer stops where it was on the screen (the line it had not finished is not painted, nor is anything still crossing from the runtime) and no `✗ system:` or summary follows; a running command is killed at the keystroke with its process group, its row `└ Cancelled <command>` and its line `▪ Cancelled <command> · …` the turn's (the UI raises the turn's own flag, which the command's supervisor polls), where a running file call runs to its end first -- **and the interrupt drops whatever was queued behind it**, said as `⊘ queue: dropping what was queued behind it as well.`, because a queue that started running a moment after the user asked everything to stop would be the surprise the band's `┋` row is there to prevent; a prompt typed *after* the interrupt is a new intention and still runs, which the interrupt is told the boundary of rather than left to guess. What the model is told about the stopped turn is upstream's: the cut-off answer closed with `The previous response ended before completion.` (or the tools that finished, named), a call the turn stopped under -- one that never ran, a command the user stopped, or a question the user cancelled -- answered `aborted by user` as a failure, then the `<turn_aborted>` user message (`session.zig:52-64`, `:2695-2747`); any failed tool result is sent as a failure (`error-text` on the Gateway, `is_error` on llmux; `vercel_protocol.zig:742-747`), and a refusal as `execution-denied` with the refusal as its reason (`vercel_protocol.zig:742-744`; `is_error` on llmux, which has no word of its own). A typed Ctrl-Z still binds nothing -- upstream's suspends (`app_input_runtime.zig:862-866@481104fd`), so here only a `SIGTSTP` stops the session; owner unit: **CTRL-Z** (queued) -- and Ctrl-D is not an exit while a turn is running. Escape is a two-tap: over a running turn a second one within a second stops it, and the hint row says `esc again to interrupt` (narrower: `esc esc interrupt`, `esc esc`) in between; at an idle prompt with a draft a second one within 500 ms clears the composer, and the row says `esc again to clear`, because nothing else in this phase discards a draft without a key that says so; at an empty idle composer Escape arms nothing (`app_input_runtime.zig:3210-3213`). A prompt submitted while a turn is running is upstream's steer, delivered into that turn at upstream's boundaries (`worker_runtime.zig:1080-1104`, `orchestrator.zig:157-261`): over a tool-free answer at once -- the answer cut where it had got to and kept as the assistant's message -- while a tool runs after its results, and with the model's answer after it, the turn going on; the model is told each in upstream's `<user_steering>` wrapper and a later turn replays the words as typed. It is the band's `┋ <prompt>` row in the dim grey until the turn takes it and its card is written then; Up at an empty composer takes the newest back while it still waits for a tool (`popQueuedSteerForEdit`); a stop drops steers with the queue, and one the turn never took runs as the next prompt. A request the turn retries (a status the server says is transient, a connection that never opened) replaces the activity row with upstream's recovery label, `⚠ Provider unavailable · HTTP 500 · server_error: upstream exploded · retrying request in 1s`, counting down to the next attempt and kept through a retried answer arriving, then `✓ recovered · attempt N` for 1.5 s or until the turn ends (`worker_status.zig:5`, `:221-235`), a label too wide for the screen wrapping onto up to three rows under its mark (`render_input.zig:406`, `:689-697`); the retry budget is xfx's own three attempts, not upstream's (re-verified against fx v0.0.16, `481104fd`, which changes nothing a retry shows: upstream's label, `recoveryLabel`, `src/core/shared/types.zig:539-606@481104fd`, differs from 0.0.13's only by an action the retry path never takes, and its count still rounds up, `format_retry_label`, `src/core/output/worker_status.zig:201-219`, `:212@481104fd` -- xfx's `src/tui/retry.rs:369-381`, `:384-391`, with its three causes at `:252-261`; the statuses retried are still 429, 500, 502, 503 and 504, `fromHttpStatus`, `src/core/agent/stream_provider.zig:480-492`, and `isRetryableModelFailure`, `src/core/agent/runtime/orchestrator.zig:4496-4501@481104fd`, as xfx's `src/gateway/mod.rs:994-995`; 0.0.16's one change on that path, `fe7925b2`, deletes a stall stop a status failure could never reach; and upstream's turn still never ends on a transient failure, backing off 250 ms, 1 s, 2 s and on to 30 s and slowing to once a minute after fifteen minutes, `src/core/agent/runtime/model_response_recovery.zig:5-9`, `:270-282@481104fd`, where xfx's three attempts wait 250 and then 500 ms, `src/gateway/mod.rs:59`, `src/agent/machine.rs:78-85`, `:199-207`), and Escape over a retry arms the interrupt because upstream pauses only its unbudgeted wait for connectivity, which xfx does not have. That decoder is the session's own: bytes in, keystrokes out, one machine fed in arrival order (the keystrokes the launch probe read with the cursor report included), with a lone `ESC` resolved as the Escape key only after 50 ms of quiet, an unfinished `CSI` waited for however long the terminal takes, an unknown sequence bounded at 32 bytes and reported as a keystroke with no binding rather than as a phantom Escape, `ESC` followed by a control byte delivered as the Escape key *and* that byte -- except `ESC DEL`, which is Alt-Backspace and deletes back over one word of word characters, as upstream's does (`composer_deletion.zig:103-136`) where `C-w` deletes back to whitespace, and `ESC CR`/`ESC LF`, which is Alt-Enter and inserts a newline (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :515-519), because replaying either would make two of them two Escapes inside the double-Esc window and throw the whole draft away, and an Alt-Enter a submit besides -- and **no** C0 byte or control scalar ever delivered as text -- a tab included, because a control that reached the composer would reach the terminal again on submit; a tab is a *named keystroke* instead, which the approval panel walks its choices with and the composer has no binding for. **Key reports are decoded in both of their spellings** -- kitty's `CSI <key> ; <mods> u` and `modifyOtherKeys`' `CSI 27 ; <mods> ; <key> ~` -- through one port of upstream's `kittyUnicodeKeyAction` (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :185-266), in its branch order: a Ctrl letter is that letter's control byte and therefore its binding, Escape, Enter with a modifier, Alt-Backspace, Alt-b/f, Super+Z and the keypad are the keys they already are here, Caps Lock and Num Lock are not modifiers, and a key **release** is never a keystroke; Super+A, Super+C and Super+X are the composer's selection (:223-231) -- select the whole draft, copy it, cut it, through the clipboard `/copy` uses, the copy silent and the cut taking the text out only once the clipboard has it (`cutSelection`, `input_selection_runtime.zig:32-42`), the selected run painted in reverse video (`input_presentation.zig:995-1021`); Alt-d deletes the word to the right; Ctrl+O opens the full transcript; and a page key, bare or held with a modifier (`pageKeyAction`, :169-173), pages whichever surface has the keys -- the full transcript while it is open, the list of an open menu or picker by the composer's row cap (`routeComposerVertical`'s `picker_delta`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:222-233@481104fd`; `routeHelpMenuMove`, `:465-483`), and otherwise the composer's caret by the same number of rows (`fx-p5-input-row-limit-41x120-at-pgdn`, `-help-pgdn`, `-slash-pgdn`, `-grown-pgup`). So do the three lists that took none until unit PAGE-KEYS-SETTINGS-COLUMN: `/settings`' menu and its Model row's list (`routeSettingsMenuMove`, `:431-463@481104fd`), the `/model ` column in each of its stages and an `/effort ` line's (`navigateModelPicker`, `:1072-1096@481104fd`), and `/mcp `'s argument completions, which fx counts as a slash list (`slashCompletionCount` over the registry's `arguments`, `command_specs.zig:578-585`, `:876-889@481104fd`) and pages by `picker_delta` (`input_completion_runtime.zig:255-260`); on each a page past either end lands on the other end, not round the list (`list_window.zig:45-53`, `settings_catalog.zig:229-233@481104fd`), and `C-j`/`C-k` move a row (`routePickerControlByte`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1093-1108@481104fd`) (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/page-keys-settings-column/`, `fx-p5-page-keys-*-24x120-*`). As fx's, a slash list -- the slash menu and `/mcp `'s arguments -- takes the arrows and a page only while the composer is one row (`capped.input_extra == 0`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:250-260@481104fd`); on a taller one they move the caret and the mark stays, while `C-j`/`C-k` walk it at any height (`routeSlashCompletionMove`, `:175-180`; `fx-p5-page-keys-mcp-24x120-wrapped-*`); `/settings`, the column and the other lists fx asks first (`routeNonSlashPickerMove`, `:230`) take them at any height. A list that hangs under an argument -- the column, `/provider`'s and `/mcp `'s -- is drawn from the argument's column while the caret is on its row and from the start of the caret's row otherwise (`projectedAnchorColumn`, `input_presentation.zig:383-386`, `visual_layout.zig:705-713@481104fd`). Over an empty `/settings` list every move goes on to the composer, as fx's does when `Menu.move` answers `false` (`settings_catalog.zig:227`): Up and Down to the search's start and end (`-settings-24x120-empty-up`, `-empty-down`). While the full transcript is up, `C-j` and `C-k` reach no list -- they are the composer's newline and kill to the end -- as fx's `routePickerControlByte` refuses them there (`composerPickerSurfaceVisible`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1094`, `:1110-1116@481104fd`); the `@` list's keys there are CTRLO-AT-KEYS's (its own row). On these lists fx's Home and End are caret moves (`shortcuts.zig:51-52`, `app_input_runtime.zig:298-309@481104fd`), as xfx's are; `C-n` is `history_next` (`shortcuts.zig:17`, `app_input_runtime.zig:403-407`) and `C-p` no composer shortcut (`shortcuts.zig:107-117`), so neither walks them on either side; and the wheel does nothing at fx's composer (`app_input_runtime.zig:1036-1040`), where xfx asks for no mouse reports. `/mcp`'s menu takes a page key as one step, as fx's does (`routeMcpHostMenuMove`, `:424-429@481104fd`). What upstream resolves to a key xfx still does not have -- a selection-extending move, spelled on a letter (Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F, Alt+Shift+B/F) or with Shift on a page key, and the all-sessions toggle (`open_all_sessions`, :253) -- binds nothing beyond the plain move; Shift-Tab, upstream's `toggle_permission_mode` (:254), is the permission-mode cycle (the `/permissions` row). A selection-extending **arrow** is the exception and is not a dead key: kitty's Up and Down (`57352`/`57353`) and the keypad's arrows, Home and End resolve through xfx's own cursor table, which drops Shift together with the selection it would have extended, so with Shift held -- alone or beside Ctrl or Alt -- the key makes the plain move it makes here unshifted (a word move for Left and Right under Ctrl or Alt) where upstream would extend a selection; the legacy `CSI 1 ; <mod> A` spelling takes the same table. Every key herdr 0.9.3 (libghostty-vt, kitty flag 1) was captured sending in that form decodes to its binding, and its `u`-form Ctrl-U and Ctrl-D are driven end to end on a real pty (`.prd/tui-phase3/receipts/2026-10-06-herdr-csi-u-keys.md`). The grammar is stricter than upstream's -- one canonical decimal spelling per field, no extra field -- and not looser anywhere. Bracketed paste is framed: `?2004h` is enabled, the bytes between `ESC[200~` and `ESC[201~` are content rather than keys -- so a newline inside a paste does not submit it, a `0x03` does not cancel a turn, and an escape sequence is not obeyed -- and anything over 1000 codepoints is shown in the composer as `[Pasted text #N, M lines]` (`1 line` for one) and expanded back into the prompt when it is sent -- the prompt's card, the turn's estimate and a steer's card are that expanded text, every line on rows of its own, as upstream's card is (`input_submit_runtime.zig:705-706`, `user_message_card.zig:348-383`, `orchestrator.zig:5482-5485`) -- expanded to the text the frame made of it, which is the pasted bytes filtered, decoded as UTF-8 with anything that is not UTF-8 replaced, and with CR and CRLF line breaks normalized to LF, rather than to the bytes the terminal sent. **A prompt's card is written in the frame that clears the composer**, whole, on a full screen too, as fx v0.0.16's 70611a91 writes one (`submit` queues the card in the `route_bytes` that takes the draft, `src/tui/shell.rs` `send`/`echo`; `p5-transcript-submit` wire, the first frame after Enter); a prompt of several lines is written whole in that frame, where fx's first frame previews only its last line until the next one writes the card (`p5-transcript-submit-multi` wire) -- one frame, a few milliseconds, and the screens after it are the same (`submit-multi-card-whole`, `scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml`). A pasted prompt taller than the screen reaches scrollback whole, every line once and in order, as the session's first prompt too (433464dd, `p5-transcript-tallfirst`). A collapsed block is an **atomic entity**: it is a *span* of the draft rather than a name to be searched for, so exactly one run of the draft is the block and a second copy of its summary -- typed, or pasted back off the screen, before it or after it -- is only ever the words it looks like. Every motion steps over it whole (left, right, both word moves, and the two vertical moves, which land in front of it rather than inside it, as upstream's land on a unit's start), so the caret is never inside a summary; every delete that overlaps it at all takes the whole of it, so one backspace at its right edge, one forward delete at its left edge, a word delete or either kill removes the block rather than damaging its name. The span moves with the text around it as that text is edited. A number a block was given is **never** given again in one session -- a removed block's number is spent -- and a line recalled from history brings its blocks back under **fresh** numbers, allocated from the same checked counter a paste spends, which is what keeps two live blocks from answering to one name; at the end of that counter a paste is refused visibly and a recall hands the line back as words and says so, rather than wrapping or reusing a number. Removing a block gives its bytes back to the budget for good: what was pasted stops being sent, and typing the same words in again is typing rather than repairing. **A paste ends with its delivery, as fx v0.0.16's does.** A delivery is one continuously readable run of input: every read until a readiness check finds nothing more -- one read or many, since a read takes at most 128 bytes -- and across the 32-read cap of one turn, which carries it into the next turn unsettled (`event_loop.zig:94-119@481104fd`). Within one delivery, input that arrives behind the end marker is taken for the clipboard's rather than the user's, and it is **never read as keys**: it is counted and dropped, a second end marker or a new paste's start included. The paste it followed is **kept**, and upstream's `! input: Input that arrived right after the paste was ignored.` is said once it has been (`paste_framing.zig:58-68,102-107,159-165`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:168-198@481104fd`, 9e0eab41; c1db919 refused the whole paste instead). So clipboard text carrying an `ESC[201~` and a newline, delivered in one go, cannot submit the composer, answer an approval or take a question's choice. **That is the whole of the guarantee.** A terminal that writes one paste with a readiness gap in it -- a moment in which the check finds nothing, which a paste longer than the pty's input queue can meet while the terminal refills it (that queue holds 1022 bytes on macOS, measured) -- ends the delivery at the gap, and the bytes after it, an embedded marker's newline included, are read as keys. fx v0.0.16 draws the same boundary (`event_loop.zig:94-119@481104fd`), and so did xfx before it kept the paste. A terminal that strips an end marker out of pasted text closes the gap at the source. The rule is the same for the composer, an amendment, a question's answer, and an approval or question taking no text (`app_input_runtime.zig:9399-9431,9686-9741@481104fd`; `p5-input-paste`, `p5-input-paste-amend`, `p5-input-paste-field`, `p5-input-paste-behind`); a capture already past its budget when the delivery ends says the input limit's sentence first and the warning after, the order upstream writes them in (`input_paste_runtime.zig:189-196`); and on the review plane the sentence is on the plane's status row, where the later of two is the one shown. A marker split across two reads is the marker, and a key in a later delivery is a key. Two edges of the frame are named rather than implied. **The 8 MiB budget is the prompt's, not the screen's**: it is measured over the draft *and* every block hiding behind a summary in it, on the decoded text rather than on the bytes the terminal sent, because a draft the user can read the whole of can be standing in front of megabytes and two ceilings of 8 MiB each would be one prompt of 16. A paste that would take that sum past the budget is refused **whole** -- nothing enters the composer and no block is kept, because a summary standing for text that was never held would submit its own words -- and so is a keystroke or a yank past it; each refusal is upstream's error notice, `✗ input: That edit exceeds the local prompt safety limit and was not applied.`, said once per run of refusals, again after an edit has gone through, and for every paste, each of which begins a run of its own (`input_limit_feedback.zig:5-42`, `input_limit_rejection.zig:12-25`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:110-127`). There is **no bound on how many collapsed pastes a draft may hold**: the 64-block cap Phase 1 needed was a bound on time, because the old name-based bookkeeping re-read the whole draft once per block on every keystroke, and a span costs integer arithmetic instead -- an edit reads the draft no times whatever it holds, and a submit reads it once. And a pasted **tab** is kept in the text and **painted**: it advances to the next tab stop -- every eighth column of the screen, the composer's two-cell gutter counted, and none at the right margin (`tabAdvance`, `visual_layout.zig:714-723`) -- and the painter writes that many spaces, so pasted indentation reaches the model and the screen alike and the caret sits on the glyph it belongs to. The composer's rows are upstream's composer layout (`visual_layout.zig:100-330`): a word moves to the next row only when it fits there, and one wider than a row breaks at the margin from the row it began on; a badge or a pasted block is one unit, never broken at its own spaces, and the text after it starts a word; spaces hang past the margin; a vertical move lands on the rightmost place at or left of the column it aims at, so in front of a wide glyph it falls inside (`TargetBuilder`, `:631-657`); and a caret after a row that fills the screen stays on its last column (`terminalColumn`, `:696-702`). A resize **is** acted on after the launch, on a monotonic deadline rather than a sleep: a `SIGWINCH` marks a 50 ms debounce and the first signal of a burst starts it, so dragging a window edge costs one re-solve per debounce however long the drag lasts; when it comes due the terminal's size is read -- through a **separate** reader from the launch's, because the two want opposite things from the same refusal: a launch has no band and must solve one from some number, so a terminal that will not say its size is answered with 24x80 there, while a running session already has a band on a screen it measured, so a refusal (and a `0x0`, which a pty whose size was never set answers *successfully*) is a reading to ignore rather than a screen to move to -- and a reading that is unchanged or that says nothing costs nothing, and otherwise the band is re-solved from the new screen -- the composer re-wrapped to the width it now has, the divider, the activity row, any pending approval panel and the hint row placed from it, the unfinished transcript line re-wrapped, and the shadow forgotten so the frame that follows is a whole band rather than a difference from a screen that no longer exists. **The reflow stops at the divider**: everything above it was written into the terminal's own document once and the terminal re-wrapped it when it changed size, by rules xfx does not model, so xfx repaints no committed row -- it writes only the band's own rows and the rows the band owned on the screen that was. A screen no band fits on re-solves nothing and, above all, answers nothing: a pending question is kept, not refused on the user's behalf because a window was dragged, and the band is re-solved when the screen grows again -- including back to exactly the size it left. **And while xfx's row numbers are claims about a screen that may not exist, it writes nothing at all** -- neither from the signal until the debounce resolves, nor for as long as the screen stays too small. A terminal answers a `CUP` past its last row by clamping it silently, so a frame written then lands on the bottom row on top of itself, and an append written then is a scroll whose rows enter native scrollback where nothing can take them back. Nothing is dropped by the wait: the rows the document is owed stay owed and the reasons stay pending, and the first frame on a screen that can hold a band carries both. **The exit is where that wait would become a loss, so it does not wait**: a shutdown with a `SIGWINCH` still outstanding measures the screen once, deadline or not, re-solves the band from it and writes the answer the pacer is still holding at the coordinates the screen really has -- nothing repaints a committed row later, so a session that came down mid-debounce would simply lose it. An exit with nothing outstanding measures nothing. The one case that still comes down silent is a screen that genuinely holds no band: there is nowhere to put the answer, and an append is a scroll whose rows enter native scrollback where nothing can take them back. During the launch it is acted on, because it has to be -- the cursor report and the window size are read a moment apart and the push above the band is computed from both, so a resize is measured again rather than aimed at a row that no longer exists -- and the check covers the push itself, not only the measurement: a scroll cannot be taken back, so a resize that lands while it is on the wire is compensated for by pushing again against the screen that now exists, which moves blank rows because everything above the cursor has already left. The last of three attempts is answered by pushing the whole screen into scrollback, which costs blank rows rather than a band drawn over the shell's output, and is re-checked like the others; a launch whose every attempt was contradicted while it was happening performs no further push against the degraded measurement and says so, opening the band on the freshest geometry it has rather than scrolling by a number it already knows is wrong -- the attempts that were invalidated may each have pushed already, which is safe in the one direction that matters and is why a fourth push computed from numbers known to be wrong is the thing withheld. That last case is the one where xfx may open its band over rows the shell wrote, and it takes a window being resized continuously for the whole launch to reach. A session that **panics** gives the terminal back as well, and only from the thread that owns it: the hook compares `ThreadId` against the one recorded when the terminal was taken, writes the abnormal restore pair *before* the default hook prints -- so the report lands on a cooked terminal instead of in a torn band -- and the process still dies nonzero. Ownership is recorded, and that hook armed, one statement **before** the `tcsetattr` that makes the terminal raw, so the startup order is mask, hook, raw, handlers, announce and no instant of it is raw with nothing behind a panic. A panic on any *other* thread restores nothing, and that is proven rather than asserted: a deliberate panic is taken on a second thread while the terminal is raw, and the terminal is read back still raw with its owner still holding it. Those rows, and the row for an initialization that fails on one side or the other of raw mode, need a build that fails on purpose: all of them run under the `fault-injection` cargo feature, which is off by default and in no shipped binary, so no environment variable can make a release fail this way. Answers are appended to the terminal's own document above the band -- CR-normalized, placed with CUP, scrolled with a literal newline -- so a row that leaves the screen is in the terminal's native scrollback and stays there after xfx exits. An append scrolls only the rows it *added*: a streamed answer that merely lengthens its last row rewrites that row where it already is, because scrolling once per delta would double-space the answer. Every appended row is painted on the screen **before** anything scrolls it off, one scroll at a time, because native scrollback is fed by what leaves the top of the screen -- an answer taller than the space above the band would otherwise push blank rows into scrollback and keep only its surviving tail, and Phase 1 never repaints a transcript, so what misses scrollback is gone. Each row is erased to the end of the line as it is written, because the scroll carries the band's own rows up into the document area and a re-wrap can make a row shorter than the one it replaces. Phase 1 repaints no transcript viewport: once a row has scrolled past the band it belongs to the terminal, and xfx never rewrites it -- which is also why an append the screen refused is dropped rather than retried, since a scroll that failed partway cannot be replayed without the risk of writing the same row into the document twice. The composer is xfx's own: arrows, Home/End, word moves, Backspace/Delete and the emacs control bindings, grapheme-aware so a ZWJ family or a combining accent moves and deletes as one unit, soft-wrapped word-aware with hanging spaces, and capped at fx's `inputRowLimit`: half of the screen less fx's four footer rows, plus one row, and that one row alone while the screen less four is four rows or fewer (`input_presentation.zig:252-255@481104fd`, `cappedInputRows` `:257-267`; `footer_rows`, `main.zig:173@481104fd`) -- 19 rows on a 41-row screen, one on an 8-row one (`fx-p5-input-row-limit-41x120-grown`, `fx-p5-input-row-limit-short-8x120-grown`). **Undo, redo and a one-slot kill ring are implemented.** `C-_` (`0x1f`) undoes and `C-y` (`0x19`) yanks; redo has no control byte upstream and none is invented here, so it arrives only as Super+Shift+Z in the two spellings upstream's own tests pin (`ESC[122;10u` and `ESC[27;10;122~`, `runtime.zig:3018,3025`), both of which are driven end to end on a real pty -- and Ctrl-Z still binds nothing. Whether a particular terminal emits those bytes for that chord is a property of the terminal and is not claimed here. The history is **deltas**, not draft copies: at most 100 entries and 1 MiB retained across the two stacks together (`edit_history.zig:5-6`), a single delta past that cap clears both stacks rather than being stored, a recorded edit clears redo and gives its bytes back, and eviction drops the oldest undo entry first. A framed paste is **one** entry however many reads it arrived in. An edit that puts a pasted-text block or an image's badge into the draft, or takes one out -- the collapsed paste itself, a deletion, a kill or a typed replacement over one, a yank that brings a badge back -- is a **boundary** instead, as upstream's structured edits are (`input_paste_runtime.zig:436-438`, `composer_replacement.zig:223-232`, `composer_kill_ring.zig:44-118`, `registered_entities.zig:270-290`): an undo after it finds nothing, so no undo or redo ever brings a block or a badge back, and the composer stays as it is with nothing said (`composer_undo.zig:252-268`, `app_input_runtime.zig:397-402`). Every whole-draft disposal -- submit, a slash command, the double-Escape, a slash completion, a changed recall -- is a **boundary**: the stacks are emptied, because every delta names offsets into a draft that is gone. `C-k`, `C-u` and `C-w` fill the single kill slot and a second kill replaces it whole (`kill_ring.zig:86-116` has no append arm); `Backspace` and `Delete` leave it alone; a kill over 1 MiB empties the slot rather than truncating it; and the slot survives a boundary, so what was killed out of a submitted draft is still yankable into the next one. A yank is itself an edit -- undoable on its own when it brings back text -- and a killed block comes back as the text it stood for, no block and no number, as upstream's kill ring holds a block's text (`capturePayload`, `kill_ring.zig:429-447`); a killed badge comes back a badge under a fresh number. Selection is still the binding upstream has that this phase does not. `Home`, `End` and the two kills work on the line the newlines delimit; the arrows work on the wrapped rows, with a preferred column that survives a short row. The prompt marker is a two-cell gutter every composer row is written into, so the text is measured against the screen less that gutter and a row is never wider than the terminal; the marker itself sits on the composer's first row, which a draft scrolled inside its window does not show. The band grows and shrinks with the draft -- the divider moves, and the rows a shrinking band gives back are erased, because nothing in this phase repaints a document row; a screen that refused those erasures is given them again on the next frame rather than being recorded as clean. A draft past the cap scrolls inside the rows it has rather than taking more, and a draft with more rows than 65535 is measured and scrolled like any other: the row counts are not narrowed until a terminal row is made. What is typed past the 8 MiB budget (`paste_framing.zig:16-35`) is refused whole rather than truncated, and refused **silently**: upstream flashes the composer and this phase does not. That budget counts what the draft would *expand to* rather than what it shows, so a draft holding a collapsed paste stops accepting keystrokes earlier than its own length suggests (see the `full-screen TUI` row's paste paragraph). `Ctrl-D` leaves only from an empty composer and is a forward delete otherwise. Submitting is a turn. A submitted prompt runs exactly the turn `ask` runs -- same provider, same registry, same permission authority, same session store -- on a worker thread, while the UI thread keeps the terminal and never waits on it; what was submitted is echoed into the terminal's document, though that echo is **not durable**: the activity row a starting turn adds takes its row from the bottom of the document and paints over it, and Phase 1 repaints no transcript row, so a one-row prompt echo is gone by the time the turn it started is under way and a taller one loses its last row. What closes the loop visibly is the answer appended under it, not the echo. One turn runs at a time and **one more prompt may wait** (a prompt sent while a turn runs is that turn's steer instead): the band shows it as its `┋` row for as long as it does -- the hint row has no queue segment, as upstream's has none (`buildStatuslineItems`, `app_render_runtime.zig:875-899`). A third submission is refused on the hint row, beside the text it is about, with the draft left in the composer and nothing sent -- rather than queued invisibly or silently dropped. The queue is exactly as deep as the channel carrying it, so whether two prompts typed in one breath are both taken does not depend on when the runtime happened to pick the first one up. What stops a turn also stops the gesture: a Ctrl-C that ended one answer is forgotten when that turn ends, so the first Ctrl-C of the next turn stops *that* turn rather than the session. Streamed text is **paced rather than dumped**: deltas are queued and released at the backlog divided by a second and a half, held between 400 and 5000 bytes a second, so a provider that sends a kilobyte in one frame and nothing in the next is still read at one speed -- and once a turn ends what is left of it is aimed at 200 ms instead, up to the same ceiling. An escape sequence is released whole or not at all, because half a `CSI` on a terminal is not half a colour but a terminal that swallows the next character it is given; and the attributes an answer left open are re-opened on the next release, because every frame repaints the whole band and a painter that ends its rows with a reset would otherwise turn the second half of a sentence plain. The release is measured on a clock rather than in whole bytes per tick -- the remainder of one tick's worth is carried to the next -- so the rate a stream really runs at is the rate, not the rate rounded down at every one of the 125 ticks a second. Nothing is dropped and nothing grows without a bound, and the bound is a number: the loop stops taking events the moment 64 KiB is waiting -- asked between events rather than once a batch, so the overshoot is one event rather than a channel's worth -- and an answer that arrived in a single frame is divided into 64 KiB pieces at the point it becomes events, each piece waiting for its own permit like every other one, so "one event" is a size rather than whatever the provider chose. The most the terminal side ever holds is therefore 128 KiB, and the channel fills behind it and parks the runtime in its send. Dividing never cuts one of the terminal's own units in half -- not a glyph, not an escape sequence -- and it costs an ordinary answer nothing, because an ordinary delta is already smaller than a piece. The single exception to the 128 KiB is a text that *begins* with one indivisible unit larger than a piece, which is handed over whole rather than cut apart or stalled on: a 64 KiB "escape sequence" is not one any terminal will honour and nothing that is really one glyph is that long, and half of a sequence is printable text nobody wrote. Nothing is dropped to achieve any of it: an event is indivisible once it has been handed over, and dropping the end of an answer to respect a buffering number is the failure this is built to prevent. **Every way out flushes what is left** -- Phase 1 never repaints a document row, so an answer still in the queue when the band comes down would be an answer the user never gets. The one exception is a reset (`/clear`, `/new`, `/reset`), which forgets it, because the rows it was going to be written on are the ones being handed to scrollback, and what the cancelled turn still sends is dropped until the runtime says the conversation is gone -- an open tool group with it, so no call of the old turn trails into the new session. Two consequences worth naming: a very long answer takes longer to *appear* than to arrive -- 5000 bytes a second is the ceiling, and upstream's is the same -- and xfx's own lines (a tool call's row, a refusal, the echo of a submitted prompt, the sentence saying why a turn ended) are written at the point the stream had reached when they happened rather than at the end of the document, so nothing overtakes the answer it belongs after. **Tool calls are shown as upstream's tool group** (`tool_group_projection.zig`): a header `● N tool calls · k read · 1 list · … · 1 failed · 1 denied` and one `├`/`└` row per call -- `Read a.txt`, `Matched **/*.rs`, `Ran <command>` with its words highlighted, `Edited a.txt +1 / -1`, `Exited 3 <command>`, `Failed <path>: <the tool's reason>`, `Denied file`, `No changes to a.txt; it already contains the requested content` for a change that changed nothing (`tool_presentation.zig:1046-1047`), `Cancelled file` and its own `▪ Cancelled … · What can xfx do differently?` line, each argument spelled as fx spells a command's row (every control `\xNN`, every mark `\u{NNNN}`; the full transcript's row says how, ledger `call-row-raw-argument`) -- one group per turn across model steps until text is written (`presentationGroupForStep`, `tool_presentation.zig:667-708`); a notice or an amendment's card written while it is open stays under it and the group keeps growing above them (`fx-approvals-40x120-cmd2-ask`), as upstream draws every call of a group at its anchor (`tool_group_projection.zig:1185-1201`, `:1295-1300`). It is rewritten in place while it is the document's last thing, a running command as its last row (`└ Running <command>`), a file change only once it is decided, and a question never; a theme report repaints its rows in the other palette's statusline and command greys (241 and 238 on a light screen), and the `▪` line's target stays 245 on both. What a command printed is not shown. An Esc at an approval stops the call it was asking about -- `Cancelled`, with its `▪` line, the moment the key is pressed -- and the turn with it, while a Ctrl-C there refuses only that call, which the group shows as `Denied`; a command that is already running cannot be stopped until it returns (the runtime reads the stop between calls), so its row waits for its real result rather than claiming a stop it did not have. The two exceptions are the pair a Ctrl-C is answered with -- the interrupt notice and the sentence saying the queue went with it -- which are written at once, because they are about the keystroke rather than about the answer and an answer to a keystroke that waited for the stream would not be one. What a **row** may carry to the terminal is a colour and nothing else: a row is placed verbatim, so `\x1b[2J`, `\x1b[?1049h` or an OSC title in one would be executed rather than drawn, and everything but an SGR is dropped from a row before it is placed -- dropped rather than turned into a space, because the wrap that measured the row counted it at no cells. A colour costs no columns and is never cut in half, in the wrap and in the clip alike, from one measurement both use. This phase still emits no colour of its own: the escape bytes of an answer are turned into spaces at the channel before any of this, so the re-open has nothing to re-open until a palette gives it something. An unfinished line stops growing at 256 rows -- the rows above that are settled, because greedy wrapping can only ever move the last one, and holding them cost the square of the answer's length to display. Quitting mid-stream cancels the turn, keeps draining and painting what the worker has already produced until the turn says it is over, and then restores the terminal -- all of it under a deadline, so a runtime that has *not* stopped by then cannot hold the terminal either: the channel is closed under it, the join is bounded, and the session log is left readable, recording that turn as `unfinished` rather than pretending it concluded. A turn whose provider has stopped answering without hanging up is exactly that case, because the transport reads its cancellation between reads and a quiet socket never delivers one. **`ask` mode asks in the band**, in upstream's question (`c1db919`, `approval_ui.zig`, `approval_screen.zig`): a file change is its review -- the changed lines numbered, five unchanged lines either side and `⋯ N unchanged lines ⋯` past them -- under a dotted rule, then `Permission needed · Review change` with `Edit · +N  -N` or `Write · +N  -N` on the right, `<path>  ·  Apply this change?`, and `Apply once` / `Apply + allow future edits to this file for this session` (or `writes`) / `Don't apply`; a command is `Permission needed · Choose one` with `Command` on the right, `Would you like to run the following command?`, and the command under `$ # terminal.exec cwd=<dir>`, spelled as upstream's `projectCommandText` spells it -- a control as `\x1b`, a non-printing code point as `\u{…}` -- and **past it**: every Unicode default-ignorable code point is named too (the soft hyphen, the Hangul fillers, the variation selectors, the tags; `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`, Unicode 17.0), where upstream shows them as they are (`isNonPrintingCodepoint`, `text_utils.zig:620-626` at `c1db919`, the same in v0.0.16), because xfx's width table counts them as no cells and a terminal that draws them wider -- tmux 3.7b gives a soft hyphen and the halfwidth Hangul filler a cell each -- would push the rest of the command past the margin, unseen and still run (the same holds for every other field the panel and the model's question lay out, also xfx's own: a path, a read's path or pattern, an MCP call and the "always" sentence name every one of them; a diff's lines, a question and its choices name every one except inside an emoji tmux, herdr's terminal and upstream all draw as the two cells xfx counts -- an RGI sequence, or `U+FE0F` after a character that takes an emoji presentation; upstream shows them as they are there too; and past the default-ignorables, every character xfx's width table counts as no cells on its own that is not a combining mark is named the same way -- a voiced sound mark, a prepended or spacing mark (so an Indic vowel sign such as `U+09BE` is named in text too), the Devanagari caret, a Hangul vowel or trailing consonant with no syllable to complete, which tmux 3.7b or herdr's libghostty-vt draw a cell or two wide -- and so is the rest of every grapheme cluster xfx counts as fewer cells than its characters take one by one, past its first character (a Khmer coeng chain, one cell to xfx and one per consonant to tmux; a run of Kirat Rai vowels), and of every cluster wider than four cells, which no row may be able to hold (a long Devanagari virama chain), so every Khmer coeng is named too; every scalar either terminal was measured drawing alone wider than xfx counts it is named wherever it stands -- `src/tui/drawn_wider.rs`, generated by `scripts/drawn-wider/sweep.sh` from all 1,111,999 scalars but the controls and surrogates, private-use and unassigned included (43 scalars since CTRLO-WIDER, which made the painter count 3,838 of the 3,881 it held as fx does; none in the private-use planes, so a Nerd Font icon is shown as it is): the two- and three-em dashes, nine emoji bases tmux draws as two cells unless an emoji selector follows, `U+FE0F` after a character that takes no emoji presentation, and the tags `U+E0002`-`U+E001F`; and so is a regional indicator outside an RGI flag (two cells to xfx and fx alone, one to tmux; a pair that is no flag four to xfx and fx, two to both terminals), so of the 676 pairs the 259 flags cross whole and the other 417 are named; and an RGI emoji crosses whole only without a skin-tone modifier -- of the 2,553 sequences xfx's table holds, measured one by one, tmux 3.7b draws 545 of the 2,030 with a modifier as four or six cells and every one without as two, and libghostty-vt none wider than two -- while a decomposed Korean syllable, as macOS writes file names, is shown as it is (the question's free answer and an approval's amendment hold only what the user types or pastes, so they are outside this) -- except in an MCP server's or tool's name, which `mcp::terminal_safe` spells a character at a time, so its jamo are named; measured against those two terminals only, so a terminal that draws an East Asian ambiguous character as two cells, or a combining mark as a cell of its own, can still draw a row wider than xfx counts it), then `1. Yes` / `2. Yes, and don't ask again for this exact command` / `3. No`; and the hint row under the band's bottom rule says what the keys do. The second file choice names xfx's grant (this tool on this file) rather than upstream's workspace file access, and under the choices, dim, is the one row upstream has no counterpart for: exactly what "always" would grant, in the permission layer's words -- the disclosure the line shell's prompt makes, which no label of upstream's shape can carry, because xfx's grant outlives the process into every later resume of a saved session. It is answered with `1`-`3` (a digit answers at once), the arrows or Tab and Enter; **Ctrl-C refuses this call and the turn goes on, and Esc cancels the turn** -- upstream's keys (`src/ui/input/runtime.zig:61`, `app_input_runtime.zig:3139-3143`), the reverse of what xfx's panel did before. **A decision xfx never received is a refusal**, and every way of not receiving one lands there: Esc, Ctrl-C, a session shutting down, a UI that has gone away, and a screen too short to show the question at all -- which is refused on the user's behalf with a row saying so, rather than painted with its choices below the last row of the screen. **That last one is asked of the surface the question is actually going to**, once the change has chosen it: the band's panel is measured against the rows the rest of the band leaves (`layout::fits_approval`) -- and a change it cannot hold there is reviewed on the plane before anything is refused -- and the review plane against its own screen, which it can put three answers on at every size a session can run on -- so a short window refuses a question the band cannot fit and does not refuse one the plane would have shown whole. **An affirmative is answerable only after a frame that really disclosed the request has landed.** A grant given against a screen the user could not read is a grant nobody made, so `1` and `2` are taken only once a frame carrying *this* request's target, its three controls and its whole always-scope has been written, flushed, and then re-checked on the following tick against the size the terminal still reports -- a `SIGWINCH` that landed inside the write revokes it until the next frame lands, and a repaint that wrote nothing can keep a receipt but can never mint one. Every request carries an id minted per question, so a keystroke left over from a question that has gone is consumed and ignored rather than read as permission. Refusing is never gated: `3`, Esc and Ctrl-C answer at every moment, ready or not, and a key spent before the frame is spent rather than queued -- an answer held until a frame landed would be a grant given by the renderer. When a screen cannot show the whole request the session says which of the two it is, where the user is looking: in the document while the band owns the terminal, and on the review plane's own status line while that plane does. **Holding this honestly is what makes both surfaces measure their rows from the request** rather than from a table of heights: every always-scope the permission layer builds carries an unconditional suffix -- the resume id of a saved session, or the note that this turn is not being recorded -- so it is three wrapped rows on an ordinary eighty-column screen, and a fixed two-row allotment cut exactly the half that says what "always" buys. Upstream's `approval_readiness.zig` is the contract; xfx's is a TUI-only state machine and `crate::permission` is untouched by it. The panel **has the focus** while it is up: a `1` is an answer rather than a character in the composer, the caret sits on the choice Enter would take, and a key it does not bind reaches nothing -- including Ctrl-D, which does not end a session that is holding a turn open waiting to be told what to do. It takes the composer's rows and the document's above them, as upstream's question replaces its footer: the bottom rule and the hint row stay where they were, and the draft the composer holds is kept, unpainted, until the answer gives the rows back. The question travels as an event and the answer on the **control** channel -- unbounded, and drained inside the turn -- so an answer cannot queue behind a prompt the turn will not dequeue until it ends, and an Esc typed at a panel both refuses the question and stops the turn behind it. **Esc and Ctrl-C are not the same refusal**, and the panel is where the difference is easiest to miss: Ctrl-C answers *this call* -- no, and the turn goes on with a prompt still queued behind it -- while Esc cancels the turn, so it travels as the interrupt's cancellation, carries the same watermark, prints the same two sentences, stops the turn and drops what was waiting. The refusal comes back *with* the cancellation rather than instead of it: the prompter is the thing parked on that channel, and it answers the question `no` and hands the cancellation on to the loop that can act on it. Nothing on the question is cut: the path, the command and the scope wrap onto the rows they need, and a choice a narrow screen clips is a choice the readiness gate refuses. **The model can also ask a question of its own, and it is not an approval.** `ask_user_question` takes the same rows of the band and the same focus and shares neither vocabulary nor authority with the permission panel: one question of the batch at a time, the model's options by **absolute** ordinal so the number on a row is the number to type whatever the window is showing, and a synthetic `Other` slot appended last -- identified by index rather than by its label -- which only opens a draft when it is taken, so a digit is an ordinal at a choice and a character inside the draft. Tab walks the batch and keeps each draft, the answers come back in question order however the batch was walked, and none of the three ways out mints an authority. Escape declines the batch: the model is told so with a byte-exact sentinel and the turn carries on. Ctrl-C stops the turn instead, and the calls queued behind the question in that step never run. Nothing shown is ever the answer: the row is clipped for the screen and it is the canonical label, encoded and never truncated, that goes back to the model. **A change whose review outgrows the band is reviewed on a screen of its own**, the surface upstream reviews every file on. Which surface a question takes is a property of the change rather than of the terminal: a **content** mutation whose review is more than twelve rows takes the alternate buffer -- an `edit_file` and a `write_file` both carry the part of the file the change touches, the changed lines and five unchanged ones either side with where they sit in the file, so the review numbers its lines, shows the ones around the change and counts the rest, however far past the 64 KiB bound of a side the change is -- and every other question -- a command, a directory, a change the band shows whole -- is answered in the band with the document still above it; on a screen too short for the band's question a change goes to the plane rather than being refused. A rule keyed on the screen's height alone would review a one-word edit on its own screen the moment somebody made their window short. What that screen renders is bounded and made inert at the permission boundary rather than at the surface: each side is escaped and then truncated at a character boundary to at most 64 KiB, with one ellipsis inside the bound, so the review carries at most 128 KiB and nothing in it can command or reorder the terminal. **One character is kept rather than named, and it is the change's own structure**: a real line break survives the boundary and the channel, and the review screen turns each one into a row, so a twenty-line change is twenty rows rather than one wrapped run and the second half is reached by walking. Everything else a terminal would act on is **named**, and named *individually*: the three whitespace controls by their own spellings (`\\t`, `\\r`, and `\\n` in the line shell's one-line quotation) and every other control by its code point (`\\u{001B}` for an `ESC`), so a payload that swapped one control for another is two screens rather than one. The review screen names the bidirectional overrides the same way, since those are neither controls nor text. fx v0.0.16's review writes fx's lossier forms instead (`\\x1b`, a tab and a single backslash kept as themselves, a trailing carriage return dropped); xfx's stays, since a change of a carriage return alone would read there as no change (`review-diff-spelling`, `scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml`), while its Ctrl-O screen, which decides nothing, writes fx's (the full transcript row). The split happens before anything is painted, so a break never reaches a row it did not end. The rendering is also **injective**, in two ways that were each a collision first: the backslash an escape spends is itself escaped, so a file whose lines really end and a file that merely contains the two characters `\\` and `n` are two different screens; and a control is named by its own code point rather than blanked to one symbol, so a file of `ESC` and a file of `BEL` are two different screens as well.
